# Runtime bridge test card

**Status: session 15 ran on 0.8.8 on 23 October 2026: photo mode's panel took clicks and held the exit; the save lock, Save anyway and the probes worked; NPC direction spawned, walked, spoke and took choices in the game's own widget; the senses agreed with the screens; the event and save-identity probes answered most of their rows ([NPC direction §8](../../knowledge/npc-direction.md#8-session-15-on-bridge-088-23-october-2026), [game events](../../knowledge/game-events.md#questions-only-the-game-can-answer), [save identity](../../knowledge/save-identity.md#session-15-results)). It found the panel over the category icon, the pin mark low in the middle, our choices over a device's screen, the actor's own reactions winning over our faces and greetings, a scene that walked backwards and spoke before arriving, and two misread senses; the game froze and the watchdog ended it at 14:41 (no bridge code on the hung threads). 0.8.9 fixed those offline and session 16 ran it on 23 October: the reaction wraps held (a smile stayed through 20 s), the panel's zone rule, the device-screen check, the pin mark at the side edge, the phone, the answer-key guard and the senses all passed; it found a walk away from V still going backwards, the pin mark clamped onto the minimap and the status bars, a spawn beside a machine standing unseen, no `dialogue.hub` records, the A7 layer check unable to tell its layers apart, `equipped` listing two clothing items, and a scene whose "bark" was its own greeting step. 0.8.10 fixes those offline, so the [session-17 checks](#session-17-checks-bridge-0810) are next. Session 14 ran on 0.8.6, session 12 on 0.8.4 and session 11 on 0.8.3 ([write-up](../../experiments/042-session-11/README.md)).**

**Who does what.** The maintainer starts MO2 and the game, loads a save, keeps the game window in front, and opens the character creator when asked (a mirror, or F12 with Character Customization Anywhere). The coordinator drives everything else through the bridge (MCP tools or the command line) and takes the screenshots. Agents never launch the game or MO2; only the coordinator stages, and only the `XF Runtime Bridge` entry in the test profile.

## Before the session (coordinator)

A staging checklist. Nothing here launches anything; the maintainer's everyday profile, frameworks and mod list are never touched.

**Run sessions as prepared batches.** A session's rows go into one schema-3 script (`projects/xf-runtime-bridge/tools/sessions/`, generated by `make-batches.ts`) that anticipates what can happen: each step has its precondition, retries, other routes, outcome branches, a fallback and a deadline, and each row ends PASS, FAIL, PARTIAL or SKIP with its evidence. The runner sets the staged `config.ini` keys each row needs (backed up, restored at the end) and restarts the game through MO2 when a value read at load changes, so order rows to batch a config (one theme, then the other). From the bridge project, with the private fixtures file (MO2 path and profile, game folder, staged mod folder, save, CET overlay key; `tools/sessions/fixtures.example.json` lists the keys):

1. `bun tools/session.ts tools/sessions/<script>.json --fixtures <private.json> --preflight` before anything launches: the staged build and its commit, every bridge method the script uses, the write classes, the config fixes and restarts the order costs, CET's key bindings, free commit, the writer seat and frame generation. Clear every blocker it names; fixes are the runner's own.
2. `--dry-run` prints the whole branch tree (each row's prelude included); read the rows that changed.
3. The run itself (the same command without either flag; `--rows <id,...>` for a subset) holds one writer connection for the whole batch and stops cleanly on a `handed_over` refusal or the commit tripwire. The report folder has `report.md` (verdicts, the detours the run took, refused key presses) and `manifest.json`. Turn Windows' Do Not Disturb on first, so no toast takes the foreground from the game.
4. Telemetry is always on: the recorder on every channel (started again after each restart), and each row's slice in `telemetry/a<attempt>/<row>/` (catch-up, plugin log lines, `overlay_state`, `bridge_info`, free commit, the game's memory and handles).
5. A significant bug ends the batch early (`--halt-on`, default all: a crash, the bridge lost, a failed draw call or the overlay stopping in the plugin log, a runner error, the same failure in three rows). The runner tears down, closes the game and writes `resume.json` with the rows left, the evidence and the command that continues. With `--loop` it then waits, game closed, for the fix and a `go` file in the session folder (or `go` typed), runs the fixtures' `restage` command, and continues as the next attempt in the same report (`--max-attempts`, default 5).

1. **Build under test.** Use the zips from `projects/xf-runtime-bridge/dist/`, built by `bun tools/package.ts` (it refuses a dirty tree or a DLL not built from `HEAD`):

   | Zip | Bridge | Use |
   |---|---|---|
   | `xf-runtime-bridge-0.2.0-writes.zip` | on, **writes allowed** | **This session**, in the test profile only |
   | `xf-runtime-bridge-0.2.0-diagnostic.zip` | on, read-only | Any other diagnostic profile |
   | `xf-runtime-bridge-0.2.0.zip` | off | Distribution default |

   **Staged build:** the autonomy batch and batch 2 (below), rebuilt from `main` at `509f599`; its zip and DLL hashes are in the [build record](#build-record-staged). What it adds to the staged zips: the -writes zip sets `allow_creator_leave = true`, and the -diagnostic and -writes zips carry `r6/tweaks/XFRuntimeBridge/xf_photo_mode_presets.yaml` (XF camera presets 7-9; the default zip doesn't). Check the -writes manifest for `"allow_creator_leave": true` and `"photo_mode_presets"`.

   The earlier branch build of batch 2 (`claude/bridge-batch2`) passed the same checks; the staged build is the `main` rebuild.

   **New in this build, watch in the next session:** the plugin log's load lines now include `evt=script.addresses_resolved … script_calls=on`; `script_calls=off` (or any answer `script_calls_unavailable`) means RED4ext's address library lacks an address the calls need: stop and send the log. The cursor hide works only in photo mode and clears itself in any other phase or after a 120 s idle disconnect. `photo_open` re-checks the game after bringing the window forward, refuses an unbound or unreadable key binding and punctuation keys, and sends only to `Cyberpunk2077.exe`. `face_rig_read` and `photo_expression_index` are new ([expression checks](#expression-checks-r1-and-r2)).

   **Build record of the first sessions** (26 September 2026, `main` at `c31156aaf29b`, clean tree: the script-call fix, RB-29..31, and every earlier review fix; `xfb_selftest --unit` OK, self-test 157 of 157, `bun test tools` 65 of 65, redscript and Lua lint passed on that build). The zip to stage is the `-writes` one:

   | Zip | SHA-256 |
   |---|---|
   | `-writes` | `56cea1a0a50a67b79515b8951e59a6a07c46a664ce639e0bf2f3069cb5673fc9` |
   | `-diagnostic` | `31abda27640d916a3836b43875546bf8c92fcce678b07dd4b4d107b15e3342c2` |
   | default | `aa20435b83d9834e3c2f6a942b7b1e77c9b366414d666b49e71517d2993d2c3b` |
   | (`XFRuntimeBridge.dll` inside each) | `b7b9ef32eb51ef95602904d6c4f86697ae5ee9edce1f3c845e7d76e461e3f824` |

   **New in this build, watch in the first session:** a photo-mode write whose value the menu then reports differently fails with `write_mismatch` and is put back (RB-19). If camera writes fail that way at step 11, note the requested and reported values; the comparison tolerance is one slider step. A light change waits three game ticks between selecting and setting (RB-20). Every game and script call now carries a caller frame and a context (the crash fix; the [script-call check](#script-call-check-first) proves it first), and `photo_camera_set {reset: true}` may answer `partial: true` with `errors` and an undo when a key fails (RB-29).

   **Staged** on 26 September 2026: the build above, unpacked into the MO2 mod `XF Runtime Bridge` (first row of the test profile's `modlist.txt`; nothing else changed), with `%LOCALAPPDATA%\XFStudio\runtime-bridge\` emptied. Baseline capture `bridge-phase2-pre`.

   A rebuild after merging gives new hashes (the DLL embeds the commit); record them here if the staged zip is rebuilt. Check the zip's SHA-256 against the build record, and that its `red4ext/plugins/XFRuntimeBridge/manifest.json` says `"variant": "writes"`, `"allow_writes": true` and the expected `commit`. The self-test (`bun tools/selftest.ts`) and `bun test tools` must pass on that commit.

2. **Stage one mod entry** in MO2 profile **`XF Studio diagnostic`** only:
   - Create the mod **`XF Runtime Bridge`** from `xf-runtime-bridge-0.2.0-writes.zip` (MO2 *Install a new mod from an archive*, name it exactly that). The zip's root is the game folder: `bin/`, `r6/`, `red4ext/`.
   - Enable it in this profile only. Never enable it in the everyday profile; the `-writes` build must never reach a profile used for play.
   - Placement, by the [placement rule](../authoring/framework-version-check.md#mo2-placement-rule) (rule 3, no related entry): the bottom of the left pane, which is where MO2 puts a newly installed mod anyway (inside the `UNCATEGORISED` section, the first row of `modlist.txt`). Move nothing else. Its files overlap no other mod, so MO2's conflict view should show none.
   - Leave the profile's other entries as they are, including **XF Eye Artistry** (the session 2 build, already staged and enabled) and the frameworks.

3. **Framework versions** (read-only `projects/xf-studio/authoring/tools/framework-check.ts` against this profile, 26 September 2026; latest releases checked on GitHub the same day):

   | Framework | Installed in the profile | Latest stable | Needed by the bridge |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | RED4ext | 1.30.0 (game folder) | 1.30.0 | The plugin |
   | redscript | 0.5.31 | 0.5.31 | The script layer |
   | Cyber Engine Tweaks | 1.37.1 (game folder) | 1.37.1 | The status label and kill hotkey |
   | TweakXL | 1.11.4 | 1.11.4 | Optional data marker |
   | Codeware | 1.20.5 | 1.20.5 | `photo.enter` only |
   | ArchiveXL | 1.27.3 | 1.27.3 | Not needed by the bridge (XF Eye Artistry needs it) |

   **Nothing needs updating.** Re-run the check if the session slips and a framework has released since.

4. **Photo-mode mods in this profile** that meet the bridge: *Photo Mode Preferences* re-applies its saved settings each time photo mode opens, so wait about 2 s after photo mode opens before the first camera change (the session scripts wait 2 s after every `game_wait` for photo mode). *Photo Mode Unlocker XL* widens slider ranges; the bridge checks values against the ranges the menu reports, so wider ranges are simply accepted. *Photo Mode Pose Selector*, *Customisable Photo Mode UI* and *Equipment-EX* add menu items; the first `photo_state` dump records them. *Photo Mode Ex* (MO2 mod `PhotoMode-EX`) **is enabled** in this profile: it clamps V's rotation (key 7) to −180…180, so the scripts' light sweep uses 150°, 165°, −165° and −150° rather than 195° and 210°, and it persists depth of field into saves, which the scripts never write.

5. **Runtime folder.** `%LOCALAPPDATA%\XFStudio\runtime-bridge\` must hold no `session.json` and no `KILL`; delete leftovers.

6. **Coordinator tooling.** In `projects/xf-runtime-bridge`: `bun install`, then register the MCP server (see the [README](../../projects/xf-runtime-bridge/README.md#mcp-server)) and restart the MCP client so it lists the `xf-runtime-bridge` tools. The CLI (`bun tools/bridge-client.ts run <command>`) is the fallback.

7. **Baseline capture:** `python tools/capture_session.py --label bridge-phase2-pre --profile "XF Studio diagnostic"`.

8. **Tell the maintainer before the session:** use a save next to a mirror (V's apartment bathroom works); make a new manual save when asked, before the first change (this profile shares the save folder, and after the first change the bridge holds a save lock until a save is loaded; the kill switch does not release it); bind the kill hotkey once the game is at the main menu (first-session step 1); the game must run in **borderless windowed** or windowed mode for captures that include overlays, and the screenshot route is recorded either way.

## Process notes for every session

Standing rules for C's rows and tools, from the sessions' friction logs (session 15 the latest):

- **Say where to look before triggering anything transient.** A line of 3 or 4 seconds, a bark or a face is missed otherwise: post the info line ("Watch her face now: …") first, then trigger it.
- **Name places by the map's icons and labels** (the shopping cart of a vending machine, the ripperdoc's cross, a bar's glass), never by a landmark C supposes is there; when in doubt, name the nearest one from `scene_read` or the map.
- **Pre-heist saves can't leave Watson** (it is locked down before the heist): put a row's travel inside Watson, or ask for a later save.
- **Bars and shops close by the hour** (Lizzie's opens at 18:00): list the places open at the session's time, or skip time first.
- **`ui_message` limits:** each choice at most 24 characters (`ask` refuses a longer one), `seconds` at most 600; check lengths before posting.
- **`game_wait` has no `timeout_s`**; give a session-level deadline instead, and probe for reconnection rather than asking (`local/tools/wait-bridge.sh`).
- **`session_wait` needs `after`** for an event that may land before the wait starts: pass the stream's `latest` from `session_events {limit: 1}`, read just before the command it waits on. Post asks only through the helper that records their id.
- **Questions that the stop key ends** can't be answered until after Reconnect: phrase them "remember, then reconnect, then answer".
- **A dialogue pick by M** is preceded by an info line, never an ask, so the answer key doesn't compete with the game's dialogue keys.
- **The answer key** works on the ink panel and, from 0.8.9, on the CET line; a press within 0.5 s of a new question is ignored. A question that wants the key says "close CET, then press F6 in the game": keys do nothing with CET's overlay open (session 16).
- **Memory: use it freely, with a tripwire** (the maintainer, session 16). A heavy row runs beside `bun tools/commit-tripwire.ts --for-s <seconds> [--on-trip "<the row's off switch>"]`; below 4 GB of the machine's free commit it runs the off switch once and exits 3, and the row stops there. When the off switch is `session.handover`, the tripwire gives it the reason as its note, so every refusal it causes and the `handover` session event read "handed over by the commit tripwire (free commit 3.4 GB under 4 GB)", not a bare `handed_over`. A climb of the game's private bytes is data to record, never a reason to skip a row.
- **An NPC scene speaks only what it writes.** A voiceset trigger (`greeting`) is the voice's own line and may be curt or warm; a scene's lines are subtitles, and its actor's face is set before she walks.

## Finish showroom checks (bridge 0.5.0)

Only with the 0.5.0 -writes build and XF Finish Showroom staged ([pipeline guide](../authoring/studio-to-mod-pipeline.md#xf-finish-showroom-a-test-mod-of-mannequin-heads), [knowledge](../../knowledge/skin-on-spawned-objects.md)). **First the fidelity check**: one showroom head beside V, both wearing the same preset under the same light; only if the head is a fair stand-in does the lineup follow. C is the coordinator, M the maintainer; tool names are the MCP names. `<s2>` and `<gb>` stand for the two showroom build folders (session 2's collection and the Glitter board), as the coordinator's staging receipt names them.

**Stage first** (coordinator, MO2 closed; the dedicated test profile only):
- **Bridge:** replace the `XF Runtime Bridge` entry's files with `xf-runtime-bridge-0.5.0-writes.zip`. Its manifest says `"version": "0.5.0"` and `"write_classes": ["photo", "world", "character", "save", "showroom"]`.
- **Codeware:** check that the profile loads Codeware 1.20 or newer, and report the version. The showroom spawns through it; nothing installs it for the maintainer.
- **XF Finish Showroom:** one new MO2 mod entry, `XF Finish Showroom`, holding both showroom archives in `archive/pc/mod/` (`xfs_showroom_4426018f…` from session 2's collection, `xfs_showroom_d11f5a7f…` from the Glitter board). Each archive stands alone and needs no `.archive.xl`. Place the entry after XF Eye Artistry; it shares no path with it.
- **XF Eye Artistry for V:** stage a build of `experiments/020-session-2/session-2.collection.json` (`--diagnostics`) from the same commit as the showroom, so that V and the heads carry byte-identical textures of the same compile. The staged session 2 build was compiled before today's finish defaults and the Shimmer grain. Record both archives' SHA-256.
- **Setting:** a disposable save in a dim interior at night, with about 4 m clear in front of V. Photo mode's lights stay off, ReShade effects off, Ultra+ off (as for session 4's repeats).

| # | Who | Do | Expect | If not |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | M, C | M loads the save; V on foot in the clear spot; the game window in front. C: `bridge_info`, `showroom_state` | `plugin_version` 0.5.0; `showroom_state` answers `codeware: true` with no pieces | `codeware: false`: C reports it and stops the showroom rows |
| F2 | C, M | `cc_open`, `cc_apply {option: "XF", label: "Gloss A"}`, `cc_confirm` (M opens the creator with F12 if `cc_open` refuses) | V wears Gloss A · as before | — |
| F3 | C | In normal play: `showroom_spawn {manifest: "<s2>", presets: ["Gloss A · as before"], anchor: "v", distance_m: 1.5}`; then `capture_screenshot {}` | One head on a black pedestal 1.5 m in front of V, **facing her**, eyes at about her eye height, with lashes and eyes and **Gloss A on its lids**; `placed` 1 and `state.pending` 0 | Facing away: yaw sign wrong, so record it and use `showroom_rotate {yaw_deg: 180}`. Floating, sunk, collapsed or at the entity's origin: the rig pose is wrong (knowledge open question 3). Pink or black skin: a missing texture. No makeup: ArchiveXL expansion or the plate. Capture each case and stop the showroom rows |
| F4 | C | `photo_open`; `world_time_set {hours: 2}`; `photo_frame {target: "face"}`; note `distance_m`. Then `showroom_spawn {manifest: "<s2>", presets: ["Gloss A · as before"], anchor: "camera", distance_m: <distance_m>, lateral_m: 0.6}` | Spawned **in photo mode**: the head stands beside V at the same distance from the camera, both facing it | Nothing appears, or `pending` stays above 0: spawning waits for the world. Then `photo_exit`, spawn with `anchor: "v"`, `lateral_m: 0.6`, `distance_m: 0.8` and turn V, and record that spawning needs normal play |
| F5 | C | `photo_camera_set {fov: 20}`; `showroom_light {manifest: "<s2>", rig: "key", target: "piece", piece: 0}` and `showroom_light {manifest: "<s2>", rig: "key", target: "v", replace: false}`; `photo_hud_hide {}`; `capture_screenshot {}` | The same key light on both faces (0.6 m apart, neither key reaches the other face by the plan's estimate; at 0.45 m one would add about 20 %): V and the head side by side, **Gloss A reading the same** (sheen, colour, edges) | Record which differs. The head's skin is a neutral tone, so compare the makeup, not the skin |
| F6 | C | `showroom_light {manifest: "<s2>", rig: "creator", target: "v"}` (replaces the rigs), capture; then `showroom_light {manifest: "<s2>", rig: "creator", target: "piece", piece: 0}`, capture | The full creator rig on each in turn: V lit like the creator's mirror screen (cyan-white rim on her left, magenta behind her right, key low front-left), then the head lit the same way in its own frame | The rig lights the wrong side: spot axes or yaw sign (record). Much brighter or darker than the mirror: photo mode's exposure (compare with photo mode exposure −1 and +1) |
| F7 | C | With the head's creator rig: `showroom_rotate {sweep: {from_deg: -40, to_deg: 40, steps: 5, capture: true, region: "full", name: "fidelity-sweep"}}` | Five frames; the highlight moves across the head's lids and the head returns to 0 at the end | — |
| F8 | M | Verdict: does the head show Gloss A as V does (F5, F6)? | Yes: the showroom is a fair stand-in, go on to L1. No: record what differs, then run L1–L3 only as a relative comparison | — |
| L1 | C | `showroom_clear {}`; `showroom_spawn {manifests: ["<s2>", "<gb>"], presets: ["Gloss A · as before", "Gloss B · skin rough", "Gloss C · all rough", "Gloss D · rough +0.12", "Shimmer · strong", "Metal ramp · lifted", "Glitter A · base", "Glitter C · size", "Glitter E · accent"], anchor: "camera", distance_m: 2.5, spacing_m: 0.6}`; `photo_camera_set {fov: 45}`; capture | Nine heads on an arc, each facing the camera, in that order left to right | Fewer: `showroom_state` names the missing ones |
| L2 | C | `showroom_light {manifest: "<s2>", rig: "key"}`; capture; then `showroom_rotate {sweep: {from_deg: -60, to_deg: 60, steps: 9, capture: true, region: "full", name: "lineup-key"}}` | Identical key light on every head (`spill_worst` 0); the sweep moves each highlight the same way. Judge whether Gloss A–D separate, whether the Shimmer grain reads as a fine sheen with sparkle rather than dots, and whether Glitter points flash as the heads turn | — |
| L3 | C | For each of Gloss A, Gloss C, Shimmer · strong, Glitter A · base and Glitter E · accent, one at a time: `showroom_spawn {manifests: ["<s2>", "<gb>"], presets: ["<name>"], anchor: "camera", distance_m: 2.2}`, `showroom_light {manifest: "<s2>", rig: "creator"}`, `photo_camera_set {fov: 9}`, capture, then `showroom_rotate {sweep: {from_deg: -30, to_deg: 30, steps: 5, capture: true, region: "face", name: "<name>"}}` | The creator rig, the camera and the sweep identical for each preset: a like-for-like set of close-ups | — |
| L4 | C | `capture_burst {region: "face", frames: 10}` on Glitter A at 0°, then again with the head at +10° (`showroom_rotate {yaw_deg: 10}`) | Glitter points twinkle frame to frame or with the turn (the Glitter board's motion question) | — |
| K1 | C, M | `bridge_kill {}`; M looks | Every head and rig vanishes; the plugin log has `bridge.kill_cleared_showroom`. M presses Reconnect | Anything left: record it and load the save |
| K2 | C, M | `showroom_spawn {manifest: "<s2>", presets: ["Gloss A · as before"]}`, then M loads the save (or `game_load {latest: true, discard_unsaved: true}`); after the load, `showroom_state` | Nothing in the world after the load; `showroom_state` has no pieces | A head survives the load: record it (static entities should not persist) |

Evidence: every answer's JSON (spawn plans, spill estimates, sweep frames), the captures (F3–F7 as a fidelity sheet, L1–L3 as a lineup sheet and per-preset strips, L4's burst manifest), the plugin log's `showroom` lines, the Codeware version, and the three archives' SHA-256.

## Recorder and taps (Borg-mode session)

**Candidate:** 0.8.14 explicit autonomous `-writes`, `allow_test_hooks=true`, pinned disposable save/build hash, existing profile/frameworks. **Not run:** offline verification does not commission engine consumers. The maintainer approves/runs the game session; no agent launch or profile change is implied. Use one persistent MCP/session-runner `CommandApi`, `--setup-asks-private` for autonomous setup. Record current build/phase, exact save identity and source-log baselines. Keep a friction log. Restore the pinned save and require zero owned resources after the rows, including failures. Do not run traffic or contact experiments in a live lane.

Start a bounded recorder before a group: `test.record.start {channels:["v","actors","camera","subtitles","voice","face","ui","contacts"],actors:["<returned aN>"],rate_hz:10,duration_s:180,byte_cap:67108864}`. Substitute actual actor handles or use `actors:[]`. The tools keep the source connection for its bounded lifetime. Keep its `run`; use `game.catchup {run:<run>}` first, then `game.catchup {cursor:<complete returned cursor>}` at the start of every coordinator turn. `detail:true` supplies retained rows. Stop/drain before the next run; source/native private files remain the evidence owner. Ring loss, missed deadlines, invalid channels, omitted compact rows and unavailable output proof are explicit, never PASS-by-absence. Keep only captures cited by the report.

| Row | Do | Required evidence / pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| RS1: an observer finds the run | On the 0.8.23 autonomous candidate, keep the writer connected and a separate reader (`--read-only`) polling `recorder_status {}` every 5 s only while its view is shown. Observe before the first run, during a writer-started bounded recording, and after a duration/cancel/load stop. Start reader `game_catchup {run:<discovered run>}` then preserve its returned cursor. | No run: `active:false`, current bridge `session`, `channels:[]`, all run-specific fields null. Active: writer's run, world, selected channels, QPC `start.s`, derived UTC millisecond `start.at`, newest `latest`, fixed `requested_hz:10`, configured duration/cap and writer `ack`; `stop_reason:null`. Stopped: same run retained with its reason. Reader discovery/catch-up neither acknowledges writer records nor starts/stops recording, takes a lease or queues game-thread work. Save timestamped writer/reader results, recorder manifest and writer command latency before/during observer polling (p50/p95/max); compare cadence/drops and report any hitch. Hooks disabled refuses `test_hooks_off` where recorder commands are registered. **Unrun.** |
| RT1 / Round 1 V | Walk a short out-and-back route, stop, and read catch-up. | Source QPC/tick/age and actual cadence; path endpoints **and distance**, health range, combat/heat, vehicle identity and busy transitions. Detail shows the route rather than interpolated NPC-cache rows. |
| RT2 / Round 1 actors and camera | Select a safe spawned actor, direct a walk/turn, move the photo camera, then read. | Fresh actor positions/command state and camera observations; no claim that attitude preparation is animation readiness. Load/disconnect invalidates the old world/run. |
| RT3 / Round 2 subtitles | Show/replace/hide a bounded line; arm a capture on `line.widget_created`, channel `subtitles`. | Producer request and actual consumer callback correlate by IDs; creation-time rect is not settled pixel proof. Capture receipt reports latency and independent visual result. |
| RT4 / Round 2 voice | Submit a covered script voice/voiceset request once. | Voice request/context/reset callbacks and coverage heartbeat; hearing/lip sync need independent output evidence. No request receipt is labelled audible. |
| RT5 / Round 3 F | Apply facial category/idle, allow a resend, reset; compare with competing ambient reaction using [R3's probe](../../knowledge/npc-face-state.md). | Submission/reset/resend source, actor, time and attitude-prepared state. Applied-input/evaluated-face/eye-bone/active-clip reads stay FOLLOW-UP unless commissioned; no conclusion about a smile from submission alone. |
| RT6 / Round 3 U | Change heading/help/answer text across HUD, menu/photo panel and CET fallback; open Save anyway offer then dismiss it. | Actual consumer text/surface/window rect, ancestor visibility/opacity and window save offer/result. Cross-check capture; owning-window rects do not claim output-pixel transforms or occlusion. |
| RT7 / Round 3 outfit | Read U in two areas with known existing wardrobe slot reads. | Per-area outfit identities agree with the wardrobe reader; missing/unknown stays explicit. Restore the original outfit. |
| BR1 / load prompt | Load fixture from main menu, then reload from gameplay. | Start screen and post-main-menu-load press are separate. Gameplay reload has no invented press-any-key; 0.8.14 reports `prompt_state:unobserved`, waits for gameplay, sends no Space. R8 native detector/continue remains uncommissioned. |
| BR2 / DM undos | Change a disposable vehicle/radio; replay each returned undo verbatim, plus other DM undos exercised in this group. | No target validation refusal for emitted `dynamic: '<digits>'`; state restored where readable. Offline suite validates every DM undo fixture. |
| BR3 / objective order | `quest.set` objectives a=`Walk to the spot ahead`, b=`Then wait here`; capture tracker, then clear. | a above b, given order; normal tracker order restored on clear. |
| BR4 / onscreen wording | `notify.show {style:"onscreen",...}` with a short label. | Game's `OnscreenMessageGameController`/`CInematic_Subtitle` placement recorded; no centring promise. A left placement is documented as game layout, not repositioned by XF. |
| BR5 / save window | Baseline total/save metadata, make exactly one approved manual save, list again. | `save_delta`: added total from `count`; rollover of the newest-20 window does not call the outgoing oldest save removed. |
| BR6 / release counts | Spawn one crate; hand over. Resume, spawn another; kill (last mutating row in that candidate). | Each removal reports one, wherever native/script/tick cleanup ran; kill log count agrees with actual disappearance. New bridge session required after kill. |
| BR7 / pending light | Spawn the industrial lamp fixture; inspect immediately, then after resolution. | Initial components pending/null rather than a false zero. `Light5050` is stored off/black; explicitly configure and verify illumination before judging output. Remove it. |
| BR8 / light logging | Run a bounded light pattern for 20 s, stop/remove. | No per-tick successful `XFDmLight.Intensity` script.call/returned flood; errors remain visible. Count lines over the exact interval. |
| BR9 / save refusal | Hold one approved bridge effect, request save without override, release. | Headline names actual holders, not every possible cause; detail matches. No unwanted save. |
| BR10 / vehicle read-back | Change a disposable vehicle's engine/doors once. | `before` and `after` returned; event-queued read-back is labelled immediate, not settled proof. Later read/capture establishes settled state; replay undo. |
| BR11 / teleport | In a clear non-road area, teleport once without an override, then walk normally; repeat with an owned movement holder active. | `held:false`, settled measured separately; no new hold until `player.stop`. Active holder refuses. Traffic status is **unknown**: human navmesh is not lane classification; Round 2 permits unknown with traffic_risk unknown and a warning; positive Road alone requires an override; SN1–SN3 commission the lane reader. No unsafe lane fixture is authorised here. |
| BR12 / contact names | `phone.state`, compare one visible contact. | Localised `name` plus original `name_key`; unresolved localisation is reported rather than silently discarded. |
| BR13 / private asks | Run a small harness session with `--setup-asks-private`, pause at an ask and end it; separately pause a public ask. | Private setup only in coordinator output. Any public pending ask is withdrawn from game on pause/end, no lingering surface text. |
| TL1 / catch-up | Let V move while coordinator waits; trigger subtitle, face/UI changes and an NPC arrival/quest objective done, then one catch-up. | All retained channels plus session events and existing digest in one call; compact omissions counted; detail agrees. Health drop/jump/combat/heat alerts checked with safe simulated/offline fixtures where runtime cause is unsafe. 0.8.15 adds partial car-hit and landing/wall controller receipts on the contacts channel; absence still proves nothing (SN4–SN7). Outwait the in-memory ring and require replay from native private chunks; bounded `more:true` continues without skipping. Missing native files/history must report a gap. Outwait the normal three-second tools idle timer and require source continuity. |
| TL2 / frame guards | Execute a safe native action with `when:{phase:"gameplay",vehicle:false,combat:false}` and `when:{spawned:[<handle>]}`; remove handle before a second request. Test `when:{photo_mode:true}` in/out of photo mode. | Matching action runs; stale/missing/unknown state refuses `precondition_failed`, error detail contains observed state and no changing step runs. Multi-step photo refusal restores its temporary selection; offline queued-state test covers state changing after submission. External key photo routes refuse `precondition_unavailable`. `game.continue` remains `no_safe_route`. |
| TL3 / event captures | Arm `capture.arm {run:<run>,event:"toast.created",channel:"ui",match:{style:"message"},capture:"screenshot",delay_ms:50,count:1,expires_s:15}`, then show a message toast. Repeat with `line.widget_created`, `npc.arrived` or `dm.quest`+quest/objective/state; exercise burst and recrop. | Read `capture.armed`: matching source receipt, bounded event-to-invocation/return latency interval and capture path. UI overlays preserved. `toast.created` covers XF MessengerNotification only. Recrop is processing an earlier image, not a fresh exposure. Wrong match, expiry, history loss, disarm, handover and tools close produce no later capture. No second bridge connection. Record queue/poll delay and source-clock uncertainty. What cancels an arm follows the feed its trigger arrives on (0.8.22, Borg 4: a `dm.quest` arm was cancelled with `source_coverage_incomplete` when the recorder missed deadlines under memory pressure). `capture_arm` answers `trigger`. An arm without `channel` (`dm.quest`, `npc.arrived`, `supervisor.*`) is a session-feed arm: missed deadlines, drops and invalid samples on the recorder never cancel it, and its receipt says what the recorder lost while it was armed (`recorder_loss_while_armed`). Only session-feed loss (`session_history_lost`) cancels it. An arm with `channel` is cancelled by recorder loss after arming, with the exact reason (for example "2 recorder row(s) dropped since arming ... subtitles"); missed deadlines cancel it only on a polled channel (`v`, `actors`, `camera`, `ui`). An arm on an incident event (`witness.*`, `incident.*`, or the `incidents` channel) is also cancelled by `incident_report_gap` or `incident_evidence_dropped`, naming the incident; other arms ignore those. Loss found by the recheck after `delay_ms` is in the capture's receipt. Under memory pressure (the tripwire running, the recorder showing missed deadlines), a `dm.quest` arm still captures. |

**R&D/performance:** measure source sampler cost/cadence, catch-up drain duration, event-to-capture latency bounds and command-queue contention on this exact hardware. Target 10 Hz source observations; report achieved rates/missed deadlines and capture delay, not a frame-exact guarantee. R3 applied-input and R4 window-to-output transforms, R8 continue ABI/lifetime and traffic/contact sensing are explicit follow-ups, not hidden failures or new unsafe adapters in this candidate.

## Safety and navigation (0.8.15)

**Candidate:** explicit 0.8.15 autonomous `-writes` build, `allow_test_hooks=true`, exact DLL/scripts hashes and pinned disposable save/profile. None of these rows has run. The maintainer runs the game; existing frameworks/mod setup stay untouched. Choose a flat, enclosed, empty off-road area with room to walk away and no ambient traffic, ledge, civilian or quest actor. Confirm full health/no combat before each contact row; stop on any unexpected damage, knockdown or approaching traffic. Keep a friction log and versioned source/private recorder evidence; reload the pinned save after cleanup. R6 §9 is the commissioning basis.

Use one persistent connection and `test.record.start {channels:["v","contacts","camera"],actors:[],duration_s:180}`. Keep the returned run, read `game.catchup {run:<run>,detail:true}` then use its **whole cursor unchanged** at every turn. Save the exact probe coordinates, recorder sequence/time ranges, callback paths, gaps/drops and observed game behavior. No synthetic callback is a physical-contact commissioning result. No lane experiment authorizes placing V in live traffic.

| Row | Do | Required evidence / pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| SN1 — placement policy / Road override | In an inspected empty off-road fixture, probe and teleport once **without** allow_road, then once with allow_road:true; read catch-up. Read-only probe a visible road target separately; do not place V in a live lane. | Current unknown proceeds with traffic_risk unknown and a plain answer/recorder/catch-up warning; no override is needed. Turn-only stays exact; teleport adds no hold. Positive Road-only refusal without override and allowance with it require RB-232's real reader; the offline truth table pins that policy. Positive CrossWalk, including overlap with Road, stays allowed with a caution. Do not claim a Road-classification PASS from unknown. |
| SN2 — crosswalk / overlaps | Probe visible crosswalk and adjacent road/pavement points, including an edge and a bridge/tunnel separation if safely visible. No V placement required. | Current response remains INCONCLUSIVE with unknown-risk warning, never invents CrossWalk from human navmesh. Unknown does not refuse placement. After RB-232: preserve overlapping Road/CrossWalk/Pavement flags, allow CrossWalk with a traffic caution (no safe-light claim), reject ordinary Road by default. Commission vertical discrimination, streamed coverage, bounds/truncation and stable lane identity before enabling classifications. |
| SN3 — nearest pavement / boundary | Probe the same target at `radius_m:2` and `radius_m:10`, plus an off-road-to-off-road segment, one road-crossing segment and points wholly inside a road polygon (read only). | Current nearest pavement stays null/INCONCLUSIVE with the requested radius. Record `road_boundary_intersection` inputs/tolerance/filter/result; **false is never off-road proof**. RB-232 commissioning must validate the suggested pavement point lies within radius, outside expanded road geometry at the right height, with walkability/clearance checks; absence, stale lanes and missing coverage must stay explicit. No duplicate-point containment shortcut. |
| SN4 — safe low-speed vehicle contact equivalent | In the enclosed fixture spawn a stationary disposable Hella away from V: `world.spawn {kind:"vehicle",record:"Vehicle.v_standard2_archer_hella_player",at:<reviewed clear point>}`. Resolve/read back its position; engine off via `vehicle.set`. The maintainer walks V slowly into its side once, then steps away; read catch-up immediately. Remove the returned spawn handle and reload. | This is the closest bounded low-speed fixture: V walks into a parked car rather than an uncontrolled driver being sent into V. Keep full health; abort on damage. Require an actual controller-report or car-hit receipt and a `contact` alert with source time/position; controller identity/speed remain null, and ordinary supporting ground is not attributed to the vehicle. If no callback arrives, mark **INCONCLUSIVE**, not no contact. A controller report without body identity does not prove vehicle attribution. A moving-car impact/early-return pair is pending SN5/RB-233, not certified by this equivalent. |
| SN5 — car-hit entry / coverage | Only after a separately reviewed, bounded slow-driver/stop fixture exists: arrange one low-speed car-to-V contact off-road, then a delivered repeat within 5 s, with protected V and immediate escape/stop capability. Otherwise leave this row pending. Also record one safe landing and wall touch with current taps. | `contact.car_hit_player` is recorded before vanilla knockdown/cooldown returns, with `carId`, source time, position and resolved vehicle **world** speed (not impact speed). Compare against actual contact; record missing low-speed, stationary, cooldown, mounted and ragdoll paths. Landing/wall data includes normals/count/truncation, may include ground, has no invented other-body ID. Unknown producer/reset coverage remains RB-233. Protection cannot turn this into a no-hit benchmark PASS. |
| SN6 — weapon values | With an ordinary firearm in a safe empty shooting fixture, sample `player.state`, `player.status` vitals, digest and recorder while holstered/safe, ready, reloading and aiming; use one maintainer-controlled safe shot and inspect transitions. | Script-backed values **Ready 5, Safe 6, Reload 2, Shoot 8; UpperBody Aiming 6** agree where sampled. A short Shoot can fall between 10 Hz samples: missing it is INCONCLUSIVE, not a different enum. Weapon action readback is immediate after queue, not settled completion; Ready is not `can_fire`. No PSM state is directly written. |
| SN7 — between-turn history / cost | After one actual receipt, wait across turns and read twice with returned cursors. Repeat stop/load/cap-loss paths; measure wall-contact report rate, recorder overhead and catch-up drain duration. | First summary says `contact observed`; subsequent interval can say `no contact observed (covered paths: …)` while run latches retain the earlier contact. Heartbeats are not contacts. Every summary keeps `commissioned:false`, `continuous:false`, `no_contact_proof:false`; gaps/caps stay explicit. Normal packages contain no contact wrappers; native counts **27 normal / 32 autonomous**. No full no-contact proof, even with complete history. |

## Lifecycle detection and continue plan (0.8.15 Round 2)

**Candidate:** exact 0.8.15 Round 2 autonomous `-writes` DLL/scripts hashes, `allow_test_hooks=true`, existing disposable profile/frameworks and pinned save. The maintainer performs every launch/load/key action; no agent game or MO2 operation is implied. Start with the read-only detector/probe; **actual bridge continue and game.quit are disabled**. This candidate can commission menu presence, loading-gate observations and dry-run plans. Original Start/UI_Skip event ABI and active GSM phase traces require separately reviewed read-only instrumentation; mark those portions **NOT RUN** when that instrumentation is absent. Keep versioned private captures, PID/command-line identity, source event cursors and a friction log. Record `game.status.lifecycle` before/after each step, including generation, age, UI thread, completeness, readiness flags, subscription/panel and consumption. Status reads remain valid while the gameplay script gate is closed; stale/unresolved data must stay unknown.

| Row | Do | Required evidence / pass condition |
|---|---|---|
| LC8151 — plain launch / Start screen | Plain launch through the established MO2 executable/profile route, without the skip switch. Read status/probe before the maintainer presses Start; capture the screen, then press once and read again. | Fresh `start_screen` corroborates the **actual** engagement-menu root/visible ancestry on an active native layer; source generation changes on leave. `start_accepting`, `gsm_phase` and `start_phase` remain null: menu presence is not GSM acceptance or pixel proof. Capture original Start owner/type/`+0x84/+0xdc`, original thread/event identity and transition for RB-234. A missing observer seed, class/layout mismatch or missing callback must be unknown, never a guessed prompt. |
| LC8152 — skipped launch | Separate launch with the complete intended argument override containing `-skipStartScreen` (`--arguments "-skipStartScreen"`; R8's adjacent `--arguments='…'` form avoids treating a leading dash as a new option). Preserve any other required arguments in the full string: MO2 replaces, not appends. Never edit its saved executable definition to try this. | Child PID/profile/actual command line proves the override. Main menu is usable without a Start press; logos/login/consent waits are recorded separately. Main-menu entry seeds the observer's negative scope; fresh complete `none` must agree with the screen. No claim that this switch removes the later save-load prompt. No second game instance. |
| LC8153 — main-menu load / initial continue | Load the pinned fixture from the main menu with a bounded game.load wait. Poll status and run `game.lifecycle.probe {generation:<current>}`. The maintainer dismisses the offered prompt once. | `loading` before **both** native gates; `initial_continue` only for the exact current initial controller with subscription and resolved visible skip panel, then `continuing` on consumed/automatic state, then no offered prompt in gameplay. Capture controller/resource/tree identity, UI thread, readiness order, original UI_Skip receipt and after-skip transition. The early load response cannot certify a ready prompt. Automatic completion must not be attributed to a player press. |
| LC8154 — in-game load / no prompt | Reload the same fixture through game.load from gameplay, then separately through pause-menu Load; fast travel if the fixture permits. Send no external continue key based on an attach interval. | No `initial_continue` unless the actual eligible initial controller/panel exists. Fresh complete `none` or an honest `unknown` during unresolved scope; never infer the answer from load origin, puppet attachment or a bare waiting boolean. Record winning fast-travel/fallback tree for the bridge's path and zero stray gameplay jump/action. |
| LC8155 — read-only plan / changed state | At each observed Start/initial prompt, probe using its generation; after a real transition/replacement, retry the old generation. Call game.continue once as a refusal control, and probe during gameplay/before readiness/after consumption. | Plan identifies only a hypothetical Start `0x62b000` or UI_Skip `0xff2bdc` consumer; `called:false`, `admissible:false`, `route_available:false`, `execution_frame:false`. Old generation refuses `precondition_failed`; unknown/nonoffered state has no candidate consumer. game.continue refuses `no_safe_route`; zero dispatch, readiness forging, synthetic input or duplicate consumption. Probe is not an executing-frame compare-and-act proof. |
| LC8156 — observer coverage / later adapter gate | Read across UI silence, lost callbacks, controller replacement, load/detach and teardown. Measure hook cadence/cost, loading-tree bounds and event counts. Leave mutating native adapter commissioning pending until original caller/event/lifetime evidence is reviewed. | Age over 500 ms, truncation/ambiguity, missing seeded engagement scope or hash/import failure gives unknown. Normal package has no lifecycle wrappers/native/hook; native counts **27 normal / 32 autonomous**. RB-234 closure requires active GSM phase acquisition and validated event ABI/thread/owner lifetime followed by one-shot compare-and-act and foreground/background stale/consumed/replaced/gameplay counterexamples. **No game.quit test/command in this candidate**: shutdown/save-flush ordering remains open. |

## Supervisor (0.8.15 Round 3)

**Source/offline, unrun.** Pin the coordinator's reviewed Round 3 commit, autonomous DLL/package hashes, 2.31 executable identity, disposable save, current compiler/plugin logs and one persistent tool connection. Native counts remain **27 normal / 32 autonomous**. Enable `allow_test_hooks`, player and each policy's own world/NPC/DM write classes. No game launch or staging is authorised to an agent by this card. Keep a friction log and record NOT RUN/INCONCLUSIVE instead of inventing coverage. Follow the [supervisor design](runtime-bridge-supervisor-design.md) for the exact versioned entry and fixed policies; replace its example point with current `player.state` position. Start a recorder including `v`, `contacts`, `supervisor`, retain its run/cursor, and use detailed catch-up for evidence. A stopped recording must not stop supervision; session.events remains its other feed.

| Row | Prepared action | Required evidence / pass boundary |
|---|---|---|
| SV8151 — quiet anchor / fight | At a maintainer-inspected quiet, traffic-free spot, arm V's `hold_position` with all four invariants, `goal:return_last_safe`, `no_contact:return_last_safe`, `not_in_combat:pause_world`, `not_on_road:handover`, `alive:handover`. Remain still for at least `safe_after_s` plus two ticks; inspect state and `supervisor.safe_position`. Only then spawn an XF hostile stand-in with existing `attitude:hostile,reactions:game` in a disposable controlled fixture; use an approved no-damage setup and keep emergency handover available. Let its own AI start the fight; do not fire a player input to manufacture it. | Anchor carries actual quiet duration, unknown traffic risk and no_contact_proof:false. V's observed combat edge produces one violation and one world.pause result, followed by cancellation; state inactive. Persistent combat does not cause a loop. If this stand-in/fixture cannot start a safe fight, mark NOT RUN and prepare a separate reviewed fixture; do not target a story NPC. |
| SV8152 — parked-car contact / return | Reset explicitly after SV8151. Establish a quiet anchor clear of the parked fixture, then arrange a deliberate low-speed nudge from an owned vehicle in the disposable no-damage contact setup (SN4), with V passive and the coordinator driving the fixture. Also try the truly stationary parked-car/body-touch case. Keep the anchor within ordinary teleport range. | A **covered positive** car-hit or controller receipt must latch no_contact, emit violation, release holds, and return V to the recorded anchor through the normal teleport/traffic gates. Capture the actual result; knockdown/busy/combat can refuse and must be visible, never silently bypassed. A stationary touch with no covered receipt is **INCONCLUSIVE / RB-233**, not a pass: this slice adds no continuous passive-contact reader. Controller ground support may conservatively trigger the same policy and must be labelled identity-unknown. No health loss is needed or assumed. |
| SV8153 — player takeover | Arm again; try mapped movement, jump/crouch/sprint/dodge, camera, attack/interact, then raw mouse/stick under a bridge look where camera actions may be consumed. Separately queue a return and move before its teleport frame. Repeat with Codeware absent only in an already-approved isolated configuration; never alter installed frameworks for this row. | First covered deliberate input invalidates ownership in its callback, not at the next 3 Hz deadline. Cancellation identifies the input; queued intervention refuses precondition_failed and never reclaims V. The supervisor listener unregisters without removing another glide listener. Record thresholds and uncovered/consumed inputs; there is no complete hardware-input claim. |
| SV8153a — stationary combat takeover | Arm separately before **stationary aim, fire and reload**, with no movement or mouse/stick-axis motion. Use the maintainer-approved empty target range/backstop for one safe shot, then reload; also exercise melee attack/light/heavy/strong/block and QuickMelee in a harmless fixture. Repeat mapped keyboard/mouse and pad triggers. Include a held-at-arm counterexample and record whether a callback is actually delivered. | Cancellation reason names CameraAim, RangedAttack or Reload (and each covered melee mapping); it must not require a raw-axis delta. Check fresh button presses and mapped trigger values, then an already-queued return that must refuse after takeover. Raw buttons/consumed notifications are still not complete hardware coverage; absent callbacks are reported, not guessed. |
| SV8154 — clear / kill / pause / load | Arm before each case separately. Clear while a set/action is waiting; kill; bridge pause; world.pause; accepted game.load; a manual load/detach. Resume/re-arm the bridge as appropriate and read state without setting a new entry. Also replace the entry while old work is queued. | Each case leaves inactive ownership, no retained anchor and no later action on the new world or new entry. An earlier pending set cannot overwrite clear. Restore cleanup still runs. No automatic resume after load/handover/pause. Capture generation/world/connection and exact refusal results. |
| SV8155 — while you were away | Read one catch-up spanning each fight/contact/action/refusal/input/cancellation above. Repeat with compact/detail, a stopped recorder and session-only receipts, missing history, and the same cursor twice. | supervisor_summary says “while you were away”, includes every retained violation/action **with result**, and the cancellation cause. `counts` and `receipts` are what is new since the cursor. A later catch-up that finds nothing new still carries the run so far: `run_totals`, `recent` (the latest 8, kept in the cursor) and the message's "this run so far". It also says how many receipts on its feeds an earlier call already reported (`already_reported`). The supervisor's own writes and refusals (Borg 4's `world.pause`) are listed under `writes`, linked to its actions by cid. A write and its action read by different calls are still paired once, because unpaired ones wait in the cursor. A cursor older than the run totals (0.8.21's shape) gives `run_totals_complete: false`, a `history_complete: false` summary and "incomplete" in the message, never complete zeros. Borg 4's summary of "no supervisor receipts" on the call after the one that reported them was a reporting gap, not lost receipts. Recorder/session copies count once even when opposite feeds arrive in different calls; the second cursor read does not repeat them. A successful goal return resolves the violation yet retains its result and undo. Old-world, pre-run and post-stop contact receipts must not latch a new run; verify their creation time, not delayed flush time. A stopped run summarises only its closed source interval; later supervision remains in raw session.events. Exceed the reconciliation budget once and require an explicit incomplete-history gap, not repeated old actions. Overflow/lost history is incomplete, not absence. Contact alerts remain observed-path evidence, never full no-contact proof. |
| SV8156 — scope / bounds / policy gates | Try NPC/spawn actor references, a method/expression as policy, missing policies, rates outside 1–5 Hz, unknown road, and a policy whose write class is disabled. Exercise only bridge-owned nearby withdrawal targets, with a picked passer-by and an owned target moved beyond 30 m as counterexamples. Stop the recorder while keeping the persistent tool owner connected. | Unsupported actor adapters and executable/arbitrary policy data refuse. Unknown road produces a note, no road intervention. With MCP `--allow read,write-player`, pause_world and withdraw_nearby must refuse **at arming**; granting only NPC or only DM still refuses withdrawal. Test the direct native envelope missing/narrow caller grant with broad config, then the complete grant. Policy permission and executing-frame range/ownership checks refuse unsafe/stale work. Only our spawned NPCs/props/vehicles within 30 m are removed; picks/lights survive. No action storm; no catch-up burst after a stalled tick. Supervision and input/contact ingress continue with recording stopped. Measure tick/read/intervention cost and source cadence; no realtime-reflex claim. |

## Borg 2 regressions (0.8.17)

**Source/offline, unrun.** Fixes for what the second Borg session found (findings of 25 October 2026, bridge 0.8.16). Offline tests cover each; the rows below are the runtime confirmation, with the `ui` channel on in a recorder run of at least a minute.

| Row | Prepared action | Required evidence / pass boundary |
|---|---|---|
| B217-1 — UI channel flood | Start the recorder with all nine channels, leave the HUD, the CET line and the CET window untouched for 20 s, then open and close the CET window once. Count `ui` rows by `data.reason`. | About one `change` row per surface at the start, then `heartbeat` rows about every 2 s per surface (not 10 per second), and a `change` row at once when the window opens and closes. Stop and restart the recorder within one second: the new run's first `ui` rows are `change` rows at once, not after a 2 s wait. Total `ui` rows over the 20 s stay near 50, not 445. `game.catchup` is `more:false` on every call. |
| B217-2 — catch-up never shows a stale latest | Restart the recorder, let it run a few minutes with a busy channel set, then make the first `game.catchup` call after walking V somewhere else. | `v.current` is where V is now (the digest sample), `backlog` and `summary` state records and seconds behind whenever `more:true`, and `v.latest_stale` is true on such pages. Paging continues to the present as before. With the digest off, `v.current` is a recorder sample marked `historical`/`stale` with its age; draining a long-stopped recording never says current. |
| B217-3 — event-armed capture across early invalid samples | Start the recorder while the game is still loading so the first seconds hold invalid samples, wait until valid, `capture.arm` on a session event the coordinator will cause (`dm.quest` with `match`), then cause it. Repeat once with a `capture.arm` on a channel event followed by a deliberate recorder drop (not required if it cannot be provoked). | The arm is not cancelled by the earlier invalid samples or the run's old loss counters; the capture fires with latency bounds. `game.catchup` `gaps` after a clean interval is empty, not `invalid_source` / `source_coverage_incomplete` forever. Recorder loss after the arm cancels only an arm on a recorder channel, with the exact reason; an arm on a session event fires and reports the loss with its receipt (see TL3). With a 400 ms `delay_ms`, stopping the recorder (or starting a new run) inside the delay cancels instead of capturing. |
| B217-4 — supervisor V reference | `supervisor.set` with `actor: {kind:"player", ref:"v"}`, then with `"V"`; `supervisor.state`. | Both are accepted; `supervisor.state` `entry.actor.ref` and receipts show `"V"`. |
| B217-5 — no false heat alert | Call `game.catchup` with a cursor whose `last_v` has no `heat` field (a hand-made cursor) while heat is unchanged. | No `heat_changed` alert and `v.changes.heat` is 0; the returned cursor carries the baseline. A real heat change afterwards still alerts. |

## Input and choices (Borg-mode session)

**[source] Prepared for bridge 0.8.13; offline only, not staged or game-tested.** The candidate uses `XFB_AUTONOMOUS_TEST_HOOKS=ON` and `XFB_ENGINE_INPUT_TESTS=ON`, `allow_test_hooks=true`, and the relevant `player`, `npc`, `photo`, `save` write classes. The engine flag is a gate, not commissioning. Normal packages omit the test declarations/module; the explicit autonomous writes package has **29 natives**, ordinary builds 27. Pin the approved clean candidate commit, DLL/package hashes, current plugin/compiler logs, exact 2.31 executable hash and disposable save/profile in private evidence. This uncommitted branch is not a package candidate. Codeware and the existing actor records are required; never install or change anyone's frameworks. Keep one input owner; no quest choices, purchases, test pulses or Windows-input fallback in this section. The unchanged `game.save.override_lock` policy remains RB-226.

**[source] Runnable consumer fixture:** from the bridge project, `bun tools/session.ts tools/testing/fixtures/input-and-choices.json --fixtures PRIVATE_JSON --out PRIVATE_REPORT_DIR`. The asset-free [fixture](../../projects/xf-runtime-bridge/tools/testing/fixtures/input-and-choices.json) supplies IC0–IC4; replace its manifest/log placeholders through the private overlay. It binds returned hub/controller/request/world IDs, captures cursors before each action, checks exact event counts over closed lossless intervals, and uses [tools/testing assertions](../../projects/xf-runtime-bridge/tools/testing/assertions.ts). No step evaluates code from data. Setup prompts are explicit remaining human dependencies: entering a real changed photo state, cancelling its confirmation and opening CET. Baseline panel: visible, `photo_menu`, modal off. Have one spare manual slot and a quiet autosave interval. No automatic replay/resume of IC4 after an uncertain activation or save acknowledgement.

| Row | Prepared stimulus / observation | Assertions that check the row | Coverage / refusal |
|---|---|---|---|
| IC0 | Read `bridge_info` and current logs before any action | `bridge_ready`: exact approved 0.8.13 commit, clean build, 29 natives, exact configured classes, no compiler/plugin error | Stop on mismatch. A zero placeholder commit must not pass. |
| IC1 — bridge-hub pick and commit | Spawn one XF actor on a clear quiet street; offer two generated lines. Arm and bind `test_input_state.xf_hub`; select index 1 with `commit:false`, then commit on the same expected hub/generation | `eventually` sees selected; `event_count` finds zero `npc.choice` during highlight-only. Then exactly one authoritative `npc.choice` with the bound hub/index/label, and exactly one matching `input.result` request/run/world with `reason:committed`, `coverage:xf_action_handler` | [source] Existing XF action handler and owner receipt. [hypothesis] Runtime delivery pending; this does not prove native engine input. Release the actor before saving. |
| IC2 — photo Esc route, modal on | Open photo mode, change a real option and verify `shown:true`, `changed:true`, no confirmation. Leave an unanswered question; set `ui_hud {modal:true}`; queue one exit event against the observed controller | `equals` checks the fixture. `event_count` requires one matching `input.photo.callback` with held true, changed true, confirmation false and one matching callback receipt; zero hide events | [source] `native_exit_event` coverage, not injected physical Esc. Never call confirmed exit as the stimulus. |
| IC3 — photo Esc route, modal off | Same still-shown changed controller, fresh unanswered question, modal false; queue once. Cancel the resulting prompt through the game's normal controls afterwards | Fixture `equals`; one generation/request-matched callback with held false, changed true, confirmation true, one confirmation-token creation and one callback receipt; zero hide events | [hypothesis] Native event delivery/confirmation awaits runtime. Photo-active alone is insufficient. Do not fake `m_anyOptionChanged`. |
| IC4 — Save anyway test hook | Leave photo mode, settle baseline saves; open CET's expanded visible XF window. Arm a fresh run and bind the current real lock offer; consume one `test_ui_activate` token | `sequence` plus exact `event_count`: consumed → queued → dequeued → native → save, each once with request/run correlation. Exactly one total `save`, matching `via:save_anyway`, `ok:true`, request/run/world; `save_delta` requires one settled new slot and no lost baseline slot; `equals` requires `game_status.bridge_save_lock` and `saving_locked` true | [source] Rendered button/action path; no ImGui hit-testing claim. Activation is not a save. If the 30-second observation misses completion, retain uncertainty and inspect; **never retry**. RB-226 remains a separate policy decision. |
| IC5 — R1 commissioning probe, read-only | Separately observe existing native receiver acquisition/teardown and inspect resolved dispatch/release/axis-zero code using the protocol below. `input_probe` is inventory only. No press, axis submission, test-input native, takeover/reset native or invented signature | Separate [read-only fixture](../../projects/xf-runtime-bridge/tools/testing/fixtures/input-r1-readonly.json): `r1_trace` loads the private SHA-256-pinned capture, checks exact executable/session, zero declared synthetic submissions, source times/threads, paired receiver/owner, implementation targets/ABI evidence and pause/handover/load/teardown records | [source] No receiver-lifecycle recorder is shipped. With no native instrumentation: **BLOCKED/NOT RUN**. A status-only/partial trace: INCONCLUSIVE. PASS means evidence completeness for engineering review, never safe ABI approval or adapter enablement. |

### R1 read-only capture protocol

**[source] Located boundaries:** hash-matched 2.31 executable `a7de82945c03e041fc7339fcf9066224d98db2f5d80fea50f7947bb350a60991`; player-manager acquisition uses virtual `+0x200`, whose returned owner has the receiver at `+0x10`; dispatch slot `+0x1f8` has receiver RCX, key-shaped uint32 EDX, physical Press/Release/Axis 1/2/3 in R8D and scalar XMM3. Release helper RVA `0x29b63f0` has native caller `0x29c3bec`; the `FakeReleaseButton` RTTI wrapper omits dispatch. These are inspected operations, **not recovered callable C++ types or lifetime guarantees**. Provenance and limits: [R1 knowledge](../../knowledge/input-and-choices.md#5-r1-general-input-dispatch-and-native-hook-points).

**[hypothesis] Probe procedure:** in a separately approved, maintainer-run disposable session, attach read-only native/debugger instrumentation to **existing engine callers** of that acquisition path. Capture the returned owner, concrete receiver type and identity, vtable target RVAs, ref-count/ownership evidence and source thread/time. Do not call the untyped manager virtual to discover its result. Inspect the resolved dispatch implementation and release helper/caller, including physical enum/key/scalar widths and axis-zero handling; record evidence excerpts or references to the private disassembly, not an asserted `abi_valid:true`. Observe ownership and teardown as the maintainer pauses, hands over and loads; establish which lifetime protects a release at each boundary. A receiver becoming invalid is a finding, not permission to reuse it. If no existing retail caller can be observed, stop INCONCLUSIVE rather than invoke a test native to create one. Physical mapping/overlap is a separate observation via the existing ink `ProcessInputEvents` boundary (address hash 673454961); ink callbacks alone do not prove gameplay delivery. Synthetic release/zero commissioning comes only after this evidence has been reviewed; **there is no synthetic submission in IC5**.

The native capture adapter is **not implemented** in 0.8.13. The card is the probe brief and the tools are the evidence verifier, not a claim that `input_probe` now records acquisition. Run `bun tools/session.ts tools/testing/fixtures/input-r1-readonly.json --fixtures PRIVATE_JSON --out PRIVATE_REPORT_DIR --no-echo` only once that external capture exists. `--no-echo` suppresses the runner's start/end UI messages, keeping this invocation read-only. Private `r1_trace` supplies `{path, sha256}`; the referenced JSON uses `schema:xfb/r1-readonly-trace-1`, `game:2.31`, the executable hash, current bridge `sid`, `read_only:true`, `synthetic_submissions:[]`, and ordered `records`. Every record has `kind`, source `t_ms`, `thread_id`, `receiver`, and `evidence` (native excerpt/reference). Required records:

- `receiver_acquired`: `owner`, `receiver_type`, `owner_member_offset:0x10`, `acquisition_rva`, `ownership_evidence`;
- `dispatch_implementation` and `axis_zero_implementation`: `vtable_slot:0x1f8`, `target_rva`, `abi_evidence`;
- `release_implementation`: `helper_rva:0x29b63f0`, `caller_rva:0x29c3bec`, `target_rva`, `abi_evidence`;
- `lifecycle` for each of `context:pause`, `handover`, `load`: `lifetime_evidence`, paired with that receiver;
- `receiver_teardown`: later source time and `lifetime_evidence`, paired with that receiver.

**[source] Evidence/cleanup:** the report retains hashed file provenance, source clock, missing fields and verdict; raw native excerpts stay private. Hashing establishes capture identity, not truth of an externally supplied record. An engineer must inspect its sources before changing the refusal. The consumer fixture withdraws owned questions, releases its actor and returns modal to the stated off baseline. The maintainer cancels any real exit prompt and loads the pinned safety save when ready; one completed save cannot be undone. Record every setup/cancellation/CET/debugger friction point and every missing observation in the private session write-up, including actual candidate/profile and whether IC5 could run. No deployment or game test was performed by preparing this card.

## DM toolkit (stacked onto session 18)

Only with the 0.8.12 -writes build built with `--allow-inventory` ([build record](#build-record-bridge-0812-branch-build-not-staged)); the stack and the profile as for session 18. 0.8.12 adds the `dm` write class ([design §3.21](runtime-bridge-design.md#321-bridge-0812-the-live-direction-toolkit), [what each command drives](../../knowledge/player-channels.md#5-the-bridges-live-direction-toolkit-0812-what-each-command-drives-and-what-was-left-out)); these rows run after session 18's Z18 to SS5r3 and ask the game what the offline tests cannot. C is the coordinator, M the maintainer; tool names are the MCP names (**restart the MCP server after the restage**: it must list `world_spawn`). Follow the [process notes](#process-notes-for-every-session): a line says where to look before anything that is over in seconds, places are named by what the map or the game's prompt shows, and every step that follows an `ask` waits for its answer.

**Before the session (C, offline, 3 min).** In WolvenKit's asset browser find two entity templates and note their depot paths: `<prop>`, an ordinary small prop with no light (a crate or a bin), and `<lamp>`, a street lamp or a lamp that gives light. Both are the coordinator's choice and are only recorded in the evidence; no card row depends on which. Check that `<lamp>` holds a light component (open it, look for a light under its components). Without such a template, rows L1 and L2 are skipped and DL-r1 is the only light row. Start `bun tools/commit-tripwire.ts --for-s 1500 --on-trip "session.handover {}"` beside the section: below 4 GB of free commit it hands the game over (everything the toolkit holds is released) and exits 3; a trip stops the section there.

**Time: about 24 minutes** (rows marked * are the first to drop). The groups run in this order.

| DR2 | C, M | Bring back the same three; M presses **Pause changes** in the XF Runtime Bridge window (CET overlay); capture; M resumes | Within a second the prop is gone, the door is back, the block is gone; changes refused while paused (`writes_paused`) | As DR1 |
| DR3 | C, M | Bring back the same three, a ringing call (`phone_call`, ring 25 s) and a light (skip without `<lamp>`); `game_load {latest: true, discard_unsaved: true}`; after the load, `world_spawned`, `quest_state`, `phone_state` | The world took them: nothing remains in the bridge's lists; no script error in the log on the load (`dm released` with reason `session_detached`, info level); the door is whatever the save says | A crash or a script error at the load: stop and keep the log: a listener left on a destroyed blackboard is the risk this checks |
| DR4 | C, M | Last: bring back a prop, the door and a tracker; `bridge_kill {}`; M looks | Everything gone at once (the plugin log has `bridge.kill_released_dm`); M presses Reconnect | Anything left: record it and load the save |

**Review regression checks (same 0.8.12 candidate/profile, after the rows above):** after hanging up a bridge call, make an ordinary call through the game phone, then hand over; the ordinary call must survive (record contact and phase). With an XF tracker active, change the tracked map marker twice and wait for each spawn completion; the XF title must survive. Repeat handover, pause and kill with a phone toast and quest toast queued, a long banner/subtitle, a tracked sound and a fired one-shot whose only micro-action is delayed: queues and lines must clear, the sound stop must be logged, and no delayed action may run after Reconnect. Also force an explicit call-end failure and a timeout-end failure: ownership must remain until acknowledgment; verify exhausted cleanup makes no more automatic calls, and an explicit retry resumes it. Fill subtitle capacity and confirm new lines are refused while existing ownership stays intact. Record every friction point. Vehicle lock changes keep saving blocked until the safety save loads; failed cleanup keeps ownership and its hold until a confirmed retry or load.

**Evidence:** each answer's JSON, the captures named above, the `dm.*` events (`session_log` of the section), the two depot paths `<prop>` and `<lamp>`, the contact ids used (neutral ones), the tripwire's last line, and the plugin log's `dm` lines.

**Friction log (the standing rule):** note every step that needed a hand, a guess or a retry: a path that had to be looked up, a target that the agent could not name, a surface it could not see without a capture; each becomes queued work.

## Session 17 checks (bridge 0.8.10)

**Ran 23 October 2026 on 0.8.10 (build `c53a965b`); results in [design §3.19](runtime-bridge-design.md#319-bridge-0811-session-17s-findings) and [NPC direction §10](../../knowledge/npc-direction.md#10-session-17-on-bridge-0810-23-october-2026).** Z1 passed (natives=27, no XF redscript error; M0: game private 16.66 GB, working set 8.75 GB, machine commit free 4.0 GB, at the tripwire's floor). N1 failed (backwards after a walk to V), N2 passed (dissolve forwards), N3 failed the smile, N4 passed on the redo, N5 passed. D1r2 passed by the coordinator's captures. A7r2: both HUD layers draw in photo mode. SS5r2 partial (every area read hidden under Equipment-EX). G6r2 skipped. G9r inconclusive: the tripwire tripped at 4.0 GB free commit and ran its off switch within seconds, so the tap barely ran; the maintainer saw the frame rate drop in the food market, a hotspot of the game's own. **M1 (three loads, bridge idle 60 s each):** private bytes 18.16 GB after the market walk, then 17.13, 17.22 and 17.36 GB after each load; working set 9.6 to 9.9 GB; commit free 5.7 to 7.3 GB. A load gives back what play added (about 1 GB) and the climb is about 0.1 GB per load; the large climbs come with play in heavy areas, not with loads or the idle bridge.

Only with the 0.8.10 -writes build built with `--allow-inventory` ([build record](#build-record-bridge-0810-branch-build-not-staged)); Codeware, TweakXL, Equipment-EX and the two XF Finish Showroom mods staged as for session 16 (no restage of those). 0.8.10 is session 16's fixes ([design §3.18](runtime-bridge-design.md#318-bridge-0810-session-16s-findings)); these rows check each one in the game, then measure the game's memory over three loads. C is the coordinator, M the maintainer; tool names are the MCP names. **The MCP server must run 0.8.10's tools** (restart it after the restage): `player_status`'s inventory part answers `clothing`, `npc_spawn` may answer `moved_for_sight`, `npc_release` and `npc_move` may answer `turns_first`.

**One restage, no relaunch. Time: about 20 minutes, then the memory row (6).** The groups run in this order; if time runs short, drop from the bottom of the Drop column, never a group marked keep. Follow the [process notes](#process-notes-for-every-session) (where to look before a transient, `ui_message`'s limits, `session_wait`'s `after`, and the memory tripwire).

| Group | What | Where | Min | Drop |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [Z](#z17-the-launch-2-min) | The launch, the version, the tripwire's first reading | The quiet street of session 16 (Little China or Kabuki, daylight, open sky) | 2 | keep |
| [N](#n17-npc-direction-9-min) | The walk away, the scene twice, a spawn at a machine, the `holocall` refusal | The street, then a vending machine with an in-world screen | 9 | keep N1 to N3 |
| [D](#d17-the-pin-at-the-edge-2-min) | The pin mark at both side edges | The street | 2 | keep |
| [A](#a17-photo-mode-which-layer-draws-3-min) | Which layer draws after a reopen | The street, in photo mode | 3 | A can go |
| [S](#s17-the-senses-and-the-event-feed-4-min) | The clothing list, the dialogue hubs, the stim tap with the tripwire | Little China | 4 | drop G9, then G6 |
| [M](#m17-memory-6-min) | The game's private bytes across three loads with the bridge idle | Anywhere (the loads are of Z2's save) | 6 | keep |

**Restage first** (C, MO2 closed): in the **`XF Studio diagnostic`** profile, **replace** the `XF Runtime Bridge` entry's files with `xf-runtime-bridge-0.8.10-writes.zip` (manifest `"version": "0.8.10"`, the build record's `commit`, `write_classes` ending in `npc`, `test_probes` listing three). In the staged `config.ini` set `[ui] answer_keys = IK_F6` and `[overlay] enabled = false`, as in session 16. Note what MO2's overwrite folder holds for the bridge.

**The memory rule is a tripwire, not a skip.** Use the memory freely; a heavy row (G9, the loads) runs beside `bun tools/commit-tripwire.ts --for-s <the row's seconds> [--on-trip "<its off switch>"]`, which watches the machine's free commit (the commit limit less the committed bytes) and, under 4 GB, runs the row's off switch once and exits 3. The first row to run it stops at that point; no row is skipped for the game's private bytes climbing.

### M17. Memory (6 min)

Session 16 measured the game's private bytes at 15.1 GB (after the first load), 16.4 GB (after a second load) and 17.9 GB (about 45 minutes and several loads later), the working set steady at 7 to 8.5 GB: the bridge's own memory was flat offline, and the climb came with loads and play. This row separates the loads.

| # | Who | Do | Expect | If not |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | C | **Three loads with the bridge idle.** Read the game's private bytes, working set and the machine's free commit now (L0); then three times: `game_load {name: <Z2's save>, discard_unsaved: true}`, `game_wait {phase: "gameplay"}`, then **no bridge call at all for 60 s** (the bridge idle; no ink panel question, no scene), then read the three numbers (L1, L2, L3). Run `bun tools/commit-tripwire.ts --for-s 400` beside it | The numbers are the data: whether the private bytes climb by a roughly constant amount per load (the game's own growth), return after each load, or step once. Free commit stays above 4 GB | A trip (exit 3): the next load is not started; the numbers so far are the result |

**Friction log (throughout).** Every point of friction met in passing, with each surface by its name.

**Afterwards:** C records each result in [NPC direction §9](../../knowledge/npc-direction.md#9-session-16-on-bridge-089-23-october-2026) (a new § 10 for session 17), [game events](../../knowledge/game-events.md#questions-only-the-game-can-answer), [photo mode](../../knowledge/photo-mode.md#session-16-on-bridge-089-23-october-2026), [ink UI](../../knowledge/ink-ui.md) and the [runtime requirements](../story-engine/runtime-requirements.md) Status column, marking each source [runtime].

Evidence: every answer's JSON, the captures named above (`local/reviews/ui-review-bridge-0810/`), `session_log {tail: 500}` and `test_events {since: 0, limit: 500}` at the end, the plugin log, and the memory readings (L0 to L3 with M0).

### M16. Memory, throughout (3 min)

Session 15 froze about 14:39 (a 120 s main-thread hang, the engine's watchdog at 14:41) with the game's private bytes at 19.8 GB and 4.8 GB of the machine's commit free. The dump shows the main thread waiting, with no time limit, for a job a worker never finished, and no bridge code on any busy thread ([design §3.17](runtime-bridge-design.md#317-bridge-089-session-15s-findings)); only a full-memory dump can name the job.

| # | Who | Do | Expect | If not |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | C | After N8 and at the end: the game's private bytes and working set, the machine's free commit; beside M0 | Private bytes within a few hundred MB of M0 after the load (N8), and no steady climb through S | A climb of a GB or more: note when it started (which group) and skip G9 |
| M2 | C, M | **If the game freezes** (the picture stops, sound loops): C at once runs Task Manager's "Create memory dump file" on `Cyberpunk2077.exe` (a full dump, 20 GB or so, to the scratch drive with room), before the watchdog's 120 s end it; then the session stops | A full dump to name the job the main thread waits on | — |
