## Combat stand-in (0.8.18)

**Source/offline, unrun.** `npc.spawn` takes two new casts, `fighter_melee` (bare fists, tier-1 Scavenger grunt: the safest first fixture) and `fighter_pistol` (the tier-1 handgun grunt; it deals chemical damage), TweakXL clones of the game's own street-gang records ([NPC direction §10](../../knowledge/npc-direction.md#11-combat-stand-ins-bridge-0818)). They are in the test profile's -writes package only (`xf_npc_actors.yaml`, actors version 3), never saved, never story characters; `npc.release`, the kill switch and a load remove them. Immortal by default, so V cannot end a test by killing one; `immortal:false` makes them mortal. A hostile one is a stand-in as before: it is handed back to its own AI when the fight starts (`npc.handed_back`). `engage:true` (with `attitude:hostile`) is the fallback: it gives V to the actor's own threat list at its first moment (the plugin log's `npc-engage` line says what happened). Use the melee cast first, in a disposable fixture with room to retreat, V healthy and the emergency handover available; the pistol cast only after the melee rows pass. Record `player.status` health before each row.

| Row | Prepared action | Required evidence / pass boundary |
|---|---|---|
| CS8181 — hostile alone engages | `npc.spawn {cast:"fighter_melee", ahead_m:8, attitude:"hostile", reactions:"game"}` with `immortal` left default; watch `session.wait` for `npc.handed_back` (reason combat) and `npc.status`. Do nothing else. | The fighter walks to V and attacks within N seconds (N = 15 to start; record the actual time from `npc.spawned` to `npc.handed_back`, and the distance closed). If it only stands and faces V for 30 s, the row fails: record its `command_state`, the `npc-engage` and `npc-reactions` log lines and go to CS8182. |
| CS8182 — engage fallback | Release the first, then `npc.spawn {cast:"fighter_melee", ahead_m:8, attitude:"hostile", engage:true}`; also once with `out_of_sight:true` instead of `ahead_m`. | The plugin log has `npc-engage ... given V as a threat (hostile attitude confirmed)`, and the fighter engages within N seconds as CS8181. The out-of-sight spawn walks in and engages although it never saw V. A `was not given V as a threat` line is a failure of the attitude step: report it. V's health, stance and position are never written by either call. |
| CS8183 — supervisor | Arm `supervisor.set` as in SV8151 (`not_in_combat:pause_world`, `alive:handover`) at a quiet spot, then spawn the hostile fighter and let its own AI start the fight. | One `not_in_combat` violation edge, one `world.pause` result, then cancellation. Persistent combat does not loop. Compare with the civilian stand-in of SV8151, which never started a fight. |
| CS8184 — the fight ends | In a second fight, `npc.release {who, how:"despawn"}`; in a third, the kill switch (`bridge_kill`); in a fourth with `immortal:false`, kill the fighter with V's own weapon. | Release and the kill switch remove the actor and V leaves combat within a few seconds (the fight does not continue against a ghost); a killed mortal fighter's body stays until released (`npc.died`, `kept_body`), V leaves combat, nothing is dropped (no loot, no weapon). |
| CS8185 — V's damage taken | Read `player.status` health before the fight and after each row (and the recorder's `v` channel through a fight of at least 20 s). | The damage V took is recorded per row (points and percent), bare fists should be small; with the default immortal fighter the fight can run long, so stop it by release. Report the fighter's level if the scaling made it hit hard. |
| CS8187 — isolated from the world | Before the fights, read the plugin log's `npc-attitude` line for the fighter (it says whether it is in `xfs_fighter` and hostile to none of the common groups, or was moved to `neutral`). Then, with a Maelstrom NPC (or any gang) standing near V and a hostile fighter engaged with V, watch the two. | The log line says isolated. The fighter does not attack, and is not attacked by, the nearby gang unless that gang is provoked by gunfire; no `npc-attitude` warning. A warning (group not `xfs_fighter`) means TweakXL's group was not registered: report it, the fighter then fights from the plain `neutral` group. |
| CS8186 — pistol (optional) | Only after CS8181-CS8185: `fighter_pistol` hostile at 12 m with V at full health and cover nearby. | It shoots from range (`player.status` health falls, `npc.status` shows it handed back); same release and kill-switch checks. Note the chemical damage status and whether it kills V quickly: stop the row at 50 percent health and release. |

## Crowd substitution (0.8.19)

**Source/offline, unrun.** `npc.substitute` replaces one plain passer-by of the street crowd with an XF actor of the same look, unseen ([NPC direction §12](../../knowledge/npc-direction.md#12-crowd-substitution-bridge-0819)); the supervisor takes up to five entries, V and the bridge's own XF actors ([supervisor design](runtime-bridge-supervisor-design.md)). This section makes each of the 11 probes of the [V17 crowd-substitution checklist](../story-engine/probes/v17-crowd-substitution.md) runnable and says which ones stay partial. The CA rows are those of [crowd adoption and identity §6](../../knowledge/crowd-adoption-and-identity.md#6-session-card-rows-maintainer-run-batch-with-the-next-story-engine-session); a probe that overlaps one runs it with the same evidence.

**Set-up.** The XF diagnostic profile with the bridge's NPC permissions on (the -writes package), Codeware, TweakXL and the actor records (actors version 3, unchanged); the autonomous candidate for the supervisor rows only. A busy street in daylight, Kabuki market if possible, a save near it, V out of combat and not wanted. `session_note` at the start of each probe, and a friction log throughout. **Evidence for every probe:** the `npc_list` / `npc_substitute` answers, the `npc.substituted` event (its `look_match`, `resident`, `hold`, `removal` and `in_view_at_swap`), `npc_status`, screenshots where the look matters, the plugin log's `npc substitution ...` lines.

**What the answers hold.** `npc_substitute` answers the new actor's handle at once. `look_match` says what matched (record relation: `clone_of_same_record`, `same_family`, `nearest_in_family`, `gender_only`; appearance; colour variant; place; heading) with `confirmed: false` until `npc.substituted`, which reports the appearance the actor really wears (`exact`, `cast_default` or `fell_back`) and a final `level` (`exact`, `close`, `approximate`). `swap.hold` is what the game said when the original was held (`detached`, `in_crowd_after`: the observation of CA2). `resident` is the key to keep: `xfr1-...`, a hash of record, appearance and spawn spot; it is ours, not the game's, and `respawn` holds the `npc_spawn` parameters for the same look at the same spot and heading. **Community NPCs are never replaced** (`community_npc`): a quest can reserve an unmarked one and the removal cannot be undone, so only lane-crowd passers-by are swapped, checked before anything is held and again just before the removal. A dry run still reads a community person's identity (`original.community`: spawner, entry, `ordinal`; `resident.key` `xfc1-...`) as a diagnostic and says `substitutable: false`; that key is for later true adoption, not for substitution.

| Row | Probe | Prepared action | Pass boundary / evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| CRS1 | 1. Read a crowd person | `npc_list {radius_m: 30}`, wait 10 s, again. Then `npc_substitute {id, dry_run: true}` on one lane walker and one sitter or smoker (a community person: the dry run reports it and says `substitutable: false`). | Each row has record, `appearance`, `variant`, `position`, `yaw` and `crowd`; note which ids repeat across the two lists (a walker may be recycled). The dry run adds `original.community` (spawner, entry, `ordinal`) for a community person and `null` for a lane walker: that is CA1 and the first half of CA6 (a diagnostic read; a community person is never swapped) (`ordinal` -1 means the person's entity id is not in the community's fixed list). Pass: the fields are read for every person; the repeat behaviour and the stub reads are recorded either way. |
| CRS2 | 2. Look match | `npc_substitute {id, dry_run: true}` for the chosen person; then `npc_spawn {cast, appearance}` from its `respawn.params`, with `ahead_m` / `side_m` to stand beside the person; side-by-side screenshot at 3 m (`capture_screenshot`, `capture_recrop`). | Indistinguishable at 3 m, or the difference recorded (face, clothes, colours). **Partial:** `npc_spawn` does not report the appearance it got; the real figures come from the `look_match` of CRS3 or CRS4. The colour variant cannot be chosen at spawn and the clones carry no props, so a person with a coloured variant, an umbrella or a phone will differ (`not_matched` lists them). |
| CRS3 | 3. Substitution out of view | Turn V away from the chosen person (or stand behind a wall), `npc_substitute {id}` (default `in_view: false`); wait for `npc.substituted`; turn back. | `npc.substituted` has `in_view_at_swap: false`, `look_match.level` and `appearance.match` (the figure for CRS2), `removal.removed: true`; the person is where they were, facing the same way, and the swap is invisible when V turns back. **Partial:** the actor stands there (the original was held, and the actor has no routine); "carries on walking in the same direction" is `npc_move` to a point 10 m ahead along `respawn.params.face`, which the engine sends, not the bridge. Whether `HideIrreversibly` leaves a ghost body or collision is read here and in CRS7 (a hidden shell the crowd system may recycle: [source], never seen). |
| CRS4 | 4. Substitution in view (expected to fail) | The same on another person while on screen: `npc_substitute {id, in_view: true}` with `capture_burst` running over the swap. | The burst shows what a player would see (a pop, a flicker, a body still there for a frame); `in_view_at_swap: true`. The default (`in_view: false`) on a person in view must wait instead: `state: "waiting"`, nothing held or spawned (`npc_status` `swaps`), then swap once V turns away; with `max_wait_s: 5` and V still looking it ends with `npc.substitute_failed {reason: never_hidden}` and the person untouched. |
| CRS5 | 5. Routine | After CRS3: `npc_move` to a place up to 30 m away through the crowd, `npc_turn` to a second actor (`npc_spawn` or another substitution), `npc_say` one line and a line back, `npc_move` on. | Paths through the crowd without sliding or collisions; the exchange reads as two people talking. **Partial:** every `npc_move` target must be within 30 m of V (the bridge's range), so a 30 to 50 m walk is two moves with V following; whether the crowd's lane walkers avoid an XF actor is what this row shows. |
| CRS6 | 6. Crowd reaction | `npc_substitute {id, reactions: "game"}` (the civilian's own reactions), then `npc_move` it along the street; the maintainer fires a weapon into the air nearby. | The actor reacts as the crowd does (flinch, flee); an actor that ignores gunfire is a finding. **Partial / hypothesis:** the clone is not a lane walker, and the crowd's flee runs through the traffic lanes ([crowd adoption §3.2](../../knowledge/crowd-adoption-and-identity.md#32-what-the-game-itself-does-to-a-crowd-member)); a non-crowd civilian may cower or run on its own AI instead. A stand-in with default `immortal` is never handed back; the police response of a shot civilian does not apply to an immortal one. |
| CRS7 | 7. Release | `npc_release {who, how: "dissolve", restore: true}` after CRS5. | The actor walks off and goes once V cannot see it and it is 15 m or more away (`npc.dissolved`); `npc.restored` follows (its `in_crowd` says whether the fresh crowd person was seen in the lane system). Pass: no ghost body, no pop seen by V, and the crowd density looks unchanged. **Hypothesis:** the restored person is the vanilla crowd record spawned non-persistent and sent `AIJoinCrowdCommand` once (one of CA4's four return routes: if it stands, record that); `restore` is off by default, so release without it first and compare. |
| CRS8 | 8. Streaming | Record `respawn` from CRS3. Walk V two blocks away until the area streams out and come back. Then `npc_release {who}` and `npc_spawn` the `respawn.params`. `npc_substitute {id, dry_run: true}` on a community person before and after the walk. | The same look as before; it reads as the same person. The dry runs give CA7's first half: the same `resident.key` and `ordinal` after a stream-out. **Partial:** the actor is spawned `alwaysSpawned`, so it never streams out by itself; the row tests that the *look and key survive in the engine's hands*, which is what identity needs. |
| CRS9 | 9. Reload | Record `respawn` and `resident.key`; `npc_release all` (a save is refused while XF actors exist); `game_save`; `game_load`; `npc_spawn` the `respawn.params`; `npc_substitute {id, dry_run: true}` on the same community person. | The same look and place after the reload; the dry run's `resident.key` / `ordinal` and the person's appearance and colour variant are compared with the earlier read (CA7's second half). The key is ours: a reload loses the actor, never the key. |
| CRS10 | 10. Scale | Five substituted actors within 30 m (the limit is five actors), each on a short `npc_move` loop. `test_record_start` with the `actors` channel for the run. | Behaviour recorded; note the count at which anything degrades. **Partial:** the bridge has no frame-time reader; frame time comes from the maintainer's own overlay, and the recorder's missed deadlines and actual rate (`test_record_read`) are the bridge-side proxy. The sixth actor or substitution is refused `limit`. |
| CRS11 | 11. Kill switch | With substituted actors live, one swap still `waiting` (an original in view, `max_wait_s` 60) and one `spawning` (`bridge_kill` right after its `npc_substitute` answer, before `npc.spawned`), `bridge_kill`. | Every actor is gone; a swap that had not finished gave its original back (`reattaches` in the plugin log); the crowd is as before. **Partial:** an original whose swap *completed* was hidden for good and marked for the crowd system to recycle: the kill switch cannot bring it back, and the street refills by the crowd's own density. |
| CRS12 | Review: community refusal | `npc_substitute {id}` on a community person (the sitter or smoker of CRS1), with and without `in_view: true`; then on a lane walker. | `community_npc` both times, before anything is held (the plugin log has no `npc substitution holds` line, `npc_status` has no swap, no handle is spent); the lane walker swaps as in CRS3. If a *lane walker* is refused `community_npc`, the stub read is wrong for that kind of person: report it with the dry run's `original.community`. |
| CRS13 | Review: giving the original back | Make a swap fail after the hold: `npc_release` the actor of a `spawning` swap before `npc.spawned`; and again with V walking more than 50 m away from the held original first (a fast vehicle or `player_teleport`). | `npc.substitute_failed` says `original_held: true` and `original_returned` true **only** if the game found it; otherwise `return_pending: true`, then `npc.original_returned` (or `npc.original_unreturned` after a minute: say how far V was). The original walks on at its normal pace afterwards. `bridge_kill` with such a person still held: the plugin log's `bridge.kill_released_npcs` shows `unresolved: []` or the ids it could not find, and `npc.original_returned` follows under the kill switch. |
| CRS14 | Review: a restored person that never joins | `npc_substitute {id, restore: true}`, then `npc_release {who, how: "despawn"}` at a spot where the crowd system will not take a spawned person (indoors, or a dead-end alley). | `npc.restored {in_crowd: false, removed: true}` within about six seconds and no body left standing; with a lane in reach `in_crowd: true` and the person walks on (CA4 route ii). No more than five wait at once. |
| CRS15 | Review: seen at the last moment | Swap a person who is on the edge of V's view; turn V towards them just as `npc.spawned` arrives. | The original is not hidden while V can see it: `npc.substituted` has `in_view_at_swap: false`, or the swap rolls back with `became_visible` (the actor removed, the original given back) after a few seconds. |

**Supervisor rows (autonomous candidate, the 0.8.15 gates).**

| Row | Prepared action | Pass boundary / evidence |
|---|---|---|
| SV8191 — arm an actor | With the test profile's `allow_write_classes` holding `npc` but **not** `player`: `npc_spawn` one actor at a quiet spot, `supervisor_set` with `actor: {kind: "npc", ref: "a1"}`, `hold_position` at its place, `alive:release`, `not_in_combat:pause_world`, `not_lost:release`, `goal:return_last_safe`; wait `safe_after_s` plus two ticks; `supervisor_state {actor}`. | The entry is armed under `npc:a1`; `last_safe` carries the actual quiet time and `no_contact_proof: false`. An actor that was never armed reads as inactive. Admission followed the actor: no player class was needed (V's own entry in the same profile is refused `write_class_disabled`). A picked passer-by (`p1`) is refused `actor_unavailable`. |
| SV8192 — return | `npc_place` the actor 3 m away (its own command, so it is a deviation from the hold). | One `goal` violation edge and one `npc.place` result back to the anchor (within the hold radius), as a `supervisor.action` receipt naming `npc:a1`; no loop. A target beyond 30 m of V refuses `too_far` and the receipt says so. |
| SV8193 — lost / dead | Release the actor with `npc_release` while the entry is armed; in another run kill a mortal one (`immortal:false`). | `not_lost`: one violation and an `npc.release` receipt (a no-op for an actor already gone); the entry stays armed until cleared. `alive`: one violation and `npc.release despawn`. |
| SV8194 — several entries | Arm V and four actors (five entries); try a sixth; `supervisor_clear {actor}` for one; then kill, handover, pause and a load in turn. | The sixth is refused `supervisor_limit`; clearing one leaves the other four armed and the connection's lease held; the kill switch, a handover, the pause and a load each cancel every entry (`supervisor.cleared` per actor); `game_catchup` lists the receipts, with `by_actor` counts. |
| SV8195 — fight | A hostile mortal stand-in (CS8181's) armed with `not_in_combat:pause_world`. | One `not_in_combat` edge for that actor, one `world.pause`, then every entry cancelled by the pause. |

## Witnessed incidents (0.8.20)

**Source/offline, unrun.** The bridge groups what the game's own systems raise (stimulus broadcasts, deaths, combat targets, reprimands, vehicle thefts, combat and fear barks) into incidents and reports the ones V saw or heard as `witness.report` ([game events §13](../../knowledge/game-events.md#13-incidents-what-v-witnessed-bridge-0820) has the sources, rules and bounds). Passive and read-only: it sends nothing into the game, so no row can change a fight. Use the autonomous candidate in the test profile, one persistent tool connection, V healthy and the emergency handover available.

**Set-up.** Start the recorder with `test_record_start {channels: ["v", "incidents", "voice"], duration_s: 600}`; read a baseline with `incidents_list` (open and kept 0, `tap: true`, `config` as the knowledge page says). After each row read `incidents_list {limit: 5}`, `test_events {kinds: ["witness.report"]}`, `session_log` (kind `witness.report`) and `game_catchup`'s `incident_summary`; the four must agree on the incident's id, kind and modes. A **friction log** throughout.

**What the fixtures can and cannot do.** The 0.8.18 fighters ([combat stand-in](#combat-stand-in-0818)) are hostile to V alone and sit in an isolated attitude group, so a fighter fights **V**, never a civilian stand-in: no stand-in can start an NPC-against-NPC fight, and an incident they make has V in it (`v_involved: true`, which is reported, not hidden). A fight between two NPCs needs the world's own (a gang and the police, a gang and a gang near Kabuki or Little China); the rows below say so. The game has no NPC thief and no stall collection: the only theft it raises is **V's** (`VehicleObject.StealVehicle`), so a `theft` incident is always V's.

| Row | Prepared action | Required evidence / pass boundary |
|---|---|---|
| WI1 — the tap is live and quiet | At a quiet spot with the recorder running, `incidents_list` and `test_record_read`. | `tap: true`; `stats.observed` stays near 0; the `incidents` channel has one `incident.coverage` row a second, `channel_stats.incidents.valid` equals `attempts`; `config` is quiet 12 s, join 35 m, see 80 m, 32 open, 20 reports a minute. |
| WI2 — a street fight (V and a fighter) | `npc.spawn {cast: "fighter_melee", ahead_m: 8, attitude: "hostile", engage: true}`; let it close and fight for 20 s; `npc.release` it. | One incident `inc-N` (not one per blow): `observations` above 1, `evidence` with `combat` (the fighter targets V) and, if blows land, `melee`/`crime`; participants the fighter (a handle `a1`, its record, affiliation, roles) and V (`is_v`); kind `assault` or `street_fight` (mutual once V hits back); `perceived.modes` has `saw`, `saw.nearest_m` under the fighter's distance; closes `quiet` about 12 s after the release and gives one `witness.report` with `v_involved: true`. Record whether `MeleeHit` and `CrimeWitness` came for a stand-in (the evidence list says). |
| WI3 — a bystander | In WI2, a civilian stand-in (`npc.spawn {cast: "civilian"}`) 6 m to the side, facing the fight. | The bystander is **not** a participant unless it screams, runs or is hit (`fear` or `voice` evidence then names it as `source` or `speaker`); the report is unchanged by it being there. |
| WI4 — an assault on a civilian | V punches a civilian stand-in with bare fists (the stand-in is immortal by default). | `CrimeWitness` with V as `aggressor` and the stand-in as `victim` (and `MeleeHit` from the stand-in): kind `assault`, `v_involved: true`, the stand-in a participant with `roles` victim. This is the game's crime-near-V; no heat is asked for or changed by the bridge. |
| WI5 — theft (V's) | Get into a patrol car or a vehicle the game flags for prevention (an NCPD-affiliated car, parked); `incidents_list` after. | One `theft` incident with V as `thief` and the vehicle (`vehicle` role, its record); `v_involved: true`; it is listed as an incident V took part in. No NPC theft can be produced: the scripts do not have one. |
| WI6 — behind a wall: heard, not seen | `npc.spawn {cast: "fighter_pistol", out_of_sight: true, attitude: "hostile", engage: true}` with a building between V and the spawn, V staying put until shots come (stop at 50 percent health, release). | A `gunfire` or `assault` incident whose first observations have `perceived.heard` (V inside the Gunshot stim's radius) and no `saw` (the ray met static geometry: the fact in `incidents_list` has no sighting until the fighter rounds the corner); `perceived.modes` is `["heard"]`; the report's `confidence` is lower than a seen one; `saw` appears only once the fighter is in view. |
| WI7 — out of range: no report | The same fighter, `fighter_melee`, `out_of_sight: true`, behind a wall, released before it comes into view. | A melee fighter's combat target makes no sound (radius 0) and the wall blocks the ray: `perceived.witnessed: false`, `stats.unwitnessed` grows, **no** `witness.report`. For a far one: any fight over 160 m from V is never built (`stats.observed` does not move). |
| WI8 — a gun battle in the world | Near a district with gangs or the police (Kabuki, Little China, a heat-4 chase if V is wanted), V out of the fight at 30 to 60 m with a clear view; do nothing for 60 s. | Incidents of kind `gunfire` / `street_fight` with participants that are gang or NCPD records (`affiliation` filled), `perceived.saw` for those in the camera's view, and `heard` for the rest inside the shots' radius; `stats.dropped_rate` is 0 or small; open never above 32. Not guaranteed to occur: record what was and wasn't seen. |
| WI9 — the four agree | After WI2, compare `incidents_list`, `test_events`, `session_log` and `game_catchup` (`incident_summary`, with the recorder on and again with the `incidents` channel off). | Same id, kind and modes in all four; with the channel off, the catch-up still lists the incident from `session.events` (`sources: ["session_events"]`) and says `recorder_channel: false`. A repeat `game_catchup` with the returned cursor does not list it again. |
| WI10 — passive | Repeat WI2 once with `incidents_list {tap: false}` first (taps off), then with the tap on. | The fighter engages in the same time and way (within noise), `npc_status` and V's `player_status` show no difference; with the tap off `stats.observed` stays put and no report appears; the frame time on the performance overlay is the same. Nothing in the plugin log says the bridge wrote an attitude, fact or stimulus. |
| WI11 — a busy street | A minute in a Kabuki or Japantown crowd, V walking, then V draws a weapon for 10 s. | Frame time unchanged by the tap (compare `tap: false`); `stats.dropped_rate` and `evicted_open` read; a drawn weapon may produce `fear`/`voice` incidents (`other`, `confidence` 0.4 or below if only barks); open incidents stay at or below 32; reports no more than 20 in any minute (more wait, marked `delayed`, and are never lost unless a `witness.gap` says so). |
| WI11b — the bounds show | After WI11, read `incidents_list` `stats` and the recorder's `incident.coverage` rows; then `game_catchup`. | `tap_admitted`, `tap_dropped_cap`, `tap_dropped_weak`, `tap_dropped_repeat`, `dropped_rate` and `pending_reports` are there and grow only with load; a drawn weapon's repeated shots show as `tap_dropped_repeat`, never as `tap_dropped_cap`; `game_catchup` lists `incident_evidence_dropped` among its gaps exactly when cap, weak or plugin drops grew since its cursor, and `incident_report_gap` only for a `witness.gap`. A report that waited arrives with `delayed: true`. |
| WI12 — barks alone | Only if WI11 produced `voice` evidence: read `evidence_score` and `perceived`. | Four barks make 1.0 and a report of kind `other`; three make 0.75 and none (`stats.below_score` grows). |
| WI13 — a police stop | Draw a weapon in a place with security that warns (a corporate lobby with guards, or an NCPD officer during a trespass), then holster. | `reprimand` evidence (`InitiateFirst`, perhaps `InitiateSuccessive`, then `ConcludeSuccessful` or `ConcludeFailed`), kind `police_stop`, an `officer` and V as `subject`, `v_involved: true`. If the game never raises `ReprimandUpdate` here, record where it does. |
| WI14 — a load | With an incident open (WI2 in progress), `game_load` the safety save; after the load read `incidents_list` and `test_events`. | The open incident closed with `closed: world_changed` and, if V had perceived it, reported once before the load took effect; nothing is carried into the new world (`open` 0); incident ids keep counting up, never reused. |

**Unknowns this card settles:** whether a stand-in fight raises `MeleeHit` and `CrimeWitness`; whether `SetNewCombatTarget` fires once or repeatedly; whether the casts (`as VehicleObject`, `as ScriptedPuppet`) accept every broadcaster owner without a script error in `redscript.log`; the rate on a busy street; whether the camera-view test agrees with what is on screen (compare WI2's `saw` with a screenshot); and whether the static ray is blocked by the thin geometry (railings, glass) a player sees through. Anything wrong goes to the friction log and a `session_note`.

## Declared incidents (0.8.21)

**Source/offline, unrun.** The game raises no NPC theft, no stall collection: the Story Engine stages its own incidents with XF actors and declares them (`incidents_declare`), so V's report carries its id and kind ([game events §13](../../knowledge/game-events.md#declared-incidents-bridge-0821) has the rules and bounds). Passive and test-gated. A silent staging is sensed only by the sight sampler (twice a second); gunshot or shout stimuli near V still give *heard*. Every row ends the incident it opened, so the next row starts clean; an id is not reused while the bridge remembers it (use a new one each time).

**Set-up.** The recorder runs with `test_record_start {channels: ["v", "actors", "incidents"], duration_s: 900}`. `incidents_list {declared: true}` reads empty, `tap: true`, `stats.declared` 0, `sight_samples` 0. After each row read `incidents_list {id}`, `test_events {kinds: ["witness.report", "witness.unwitnessed", "witness.gap"]}`, `session_log` and `game_catchup`'s `incident_summary`; they agree on the incident, the engine's id and kind, and the reason.

**The pickpocketing fixture (DI2 to DI5).** Two friendly XF actors: `npc_spawn {cast: "woman_corpo", ahead_m: 7, side_m: -1.5, label: "Mark"}` is **a2**, the mark, and `npc_spawn {cast: "man_lowlife", ahead_m: 7, side_m: 2.5, label: "Thief"}` is **a1**, the thief, both facing V, both on walkable ground with a clear line to V's eyes (`scene_report` says the face is not blocked). `incidents_declare {id: "pp-<n>", kind: "pickpocket", roles: {a1: "thief", a2: "mark"}, ttl_s: 45}`. Then **the take**: `npc_move {who: "a1", to: "a2", stop_m: 0.8}`; on `npc.arrived`, `npc_say {who: "a1", text: "(lifts the wallet)", style: "overhead", seconds: 3}` and `npc_turn {who: "a1", to: "v"}`; wait 4 s; `incidents_close {id}`. Nothing in the fixture is loud: no bark, no shot, no blow.

| Row | Prepared action | Required evidence / pass boundary |
|---|---|---|
| DI1 — refusals (no actors needed) | `incidents_declare` with `roles: {v: "x"}`, `{p1: "x"}`, `{a9: "x"}` (no such actor), an `id` with a quote, a `kind` with a newline, `ttl_s: 0` and `ttl_s: 301`; then a good one twice. | `bad_params` for V, the pick and the texts (the message says why), `npc_not_found` for a9, `bad_params` for the TTLs, `duplicate_id` the second time; nothing opened by a refusal (`incidents_list {declared: true}` shows only the good one); close it. |
| DI2 — the pickpocketing, seen | The fixture, V standing still looking at the two. | One `witness.report` (and no other incident: `incidents_list {state: "open"}` and `{state: "closed"}` show only this one): `declared.id` `pp-<n>`, `declared.kind` `pickpocket`, `declared.roles` `{a1: thief, a2: mark}`, `kind` `other`, `perceived.modes` `["saw"]`, `perceived.sight.in_view` of about 2 a second over the fixture, `sight.samples` about 2 per second of simulation time, `closed` `engine_close`, participants `a1` and `a2` with their entity ids and the `who` handles, `place.last` near a2. The answer of `incidents_close` carries the same report; test_events, session_log and the recorder each hold it once. |
| DI3 — V turned away | The same fixture with a new id, but `player_look` (or `behave_look`) turns V 180 degrees **before** the declare and keeps her away until the close. | One `witness.unwitnessed` event with `reason: not_in_view`, `declared` as in DI2, `sight.in_range` above 0 and `sight.in_view` 0; **no** `witness.report`; `stats.declared_unwitnessed` grows by 1. Her back to the actors, nothing else changed: the actors behaved the same (arrived, spoke). |
| DI4 — behind a corner | The fixture placed behind a wall or a corner from V's position (V's camera pointing at the wall: the actors are in the view cone's range but blocked), `scene_report` to confirm the blocked face. | `witness.unwitnessed` with `reason: occluded` (`sight.blocked` above 0), or, if the ray is clear for a part, `saw` with fewer `in_view` samples than samples. If it reads `unchecked`, record the `sight` counters: the ray did not cast. |
| DI5 — turned back | The DI3 fixture, but V turns to face the actors about 2 s into the take. | `witness.report` with `perceived.saw.first_sim_s` after the start of the incident by about the turn time, `sight.samples` larger than `sight.in_view` (the early ones were not in view); `nearest_m` the distance at the turn. |
| DI6 — joining | During DI2, once the take has begun: play an allow-listed bark from a2 if one of `npc_say`'s triggers is in the tap's list (`fear_beg`, `fear_run`); else `npc_face` a2 afraid and note that no bark came. | The bark joins the declared incident (`evidence.voice` 1) and **no** new `other` incident opens; with no bark the incident is as DI2 (record that). A stranger's shout or shot 10 to 20 m away (a vanilla passer-by's scream) opens its own incident, not this one. |
| DI7 — the spawn link | With a declared incident open: `npc_spawn {cast: "man_corpo", ahead_m: 6, side_m: 5, incident: "pp-<n>", incident_role: "lookout"}`; then `npc_substitute {look_at: true, incident: "pp-<n>"}` on a crowd person V looks at. | The answers carry `incident: {incident, id, kind, role, who}`; `incidents_list {id}` shows `declared.roles` with the new handles (`lookout` and `participant`) and the participants; `npc_spawn` with an id that is not open: `incident_unknown` and **nothing spawned** (`npc_status` unchanged). A sixth actor in one incident is `incident_limit`. Close it: the report lists every role. |
| DI8 — TTL, on the simulation clock | Declare with `ttl_s: 8` (a silent, unwatched staging), do nothing. Repeat with `ttl_s: 30` and pause the world (`world_pause`) for 20 s, then resume. | The first closes by itself at about 8 s of play with `closed: ttl`, as `witness.report` if V was looking, else `witness.unwitnessed`. The second stays open through the pause (the sim clock does not move: `incidents_list` `sim_s` frozen) and closes 30 s of play after the declare. |
| DI9 — kill, load and handover | One declared incident open each time: `session_handover`; then `game_load` the safety save; then, last, `bridge_kill`. | `closed: handover` (and the handover's answer has `incidents_closed.closed: 1`), then `closed: load` (the incident closes before the new world counts; nothing carries across), then `closed: kill` within a tick; each is a report or an unwitnessed event, exactly once. Ordinary incidents in the same worlds are unaffected by the handover and the kill (they follow their own rules); after the load all close with `world_changed` or `load`. |
| DI10 — the bounds | Declare four incidents (one actor each, or the same two), then a fifth. Watch `incidents_list` `stats` over a 30 s staging. | The fifth is refused `incident_limit`; `sight_samples` grows by about 2 a second, never more than 3; `sight_dropped_rate` stays 0 (the script sends 2, the plugin takes 3); open incidents stay at or below 32; no frame-time change on the performance overlay compared with the taps on and nothing declared. |
| DI11 — passive | DI2 once with `incidents_declare` and once without declaring at all (the same moves). | The actors arrive in the same time and speak the same line (within noise); `npc_status` and `player_status` show no difference; the plugin log has no bridge write caused by the sampler (it only reads); the frame time is the same. |
| DI12 — the four agree, and the digest | After DI2 and DI3: `test_events`, `session_log`, the recorder's `incidents` channel and `game_catchup`. | The same incident id, the engine's id and kind in all four; `incident_summary.declared` is 1 and `incident_summary.unwitnessed` lists the DI3 incident with `reason: not_in_view` and `declared.id`; with the `incidents` channel off the session events still hold both. |

**Unknowns this card settles:** whether a half-second sample misses a short sight (DI5's `first_sim_s` against the actual turn); whether `FindEntityByID` finds an actor before it has appeared (expected: no; counted as `actors_missing`); whether an XF actor's head slot and position are what the view cone expects while it plays a scripted walk; whether the registry's handle reaches a tap observation for an XF actor's bark or scream (DI6); whether `scene_report`'s occlusion agrees with the sampler's ray (DI4); and what the extra half-second poll costs a frame (DI10, DI11).

**Friction log prompts for the session.** Anything that needed a manual step the bridge could have taken (placing the fixture, timing the take against the TTL, turning V), anything the report lacked that the engine needs (the time of the take, who was nearest), and any reading that was ambiguous.

## In-game styling (0.8.22)

**Source/offline, unrun.** The bridge's three surfaces dressed from the Studio's table ([style guide, "In-game surfaces"](../../projects/xf-studio/authoring/src/studio-ui/style-guide/in-game.ts), data in `in-game-theme.ts`; the page [ink UI §8](../../knowledge/ink-ui.md#8-an-xf-imgui-host-beside-cet) cross-references it). The rows are the runtime confirmation and, for XF · Studio's UI review, the capture list: dark and light, the overlay panel (the XF box), the CET window and the CET line, and the ink panel's answer flash, each with motion reduced and full. Offline tests cover the generator, the table's coverage of the pinned ImGui 1.92.9 enum, the motion setting, the pulse and settle arithmetic, the chip labels, the CET layer's font scales and cards under fengari (also against CET 1.37.1's own binding table), and the ink flash's timing; nothing here has been seen in the game.

**Set-up.** The test profile with `[overlay] enabled = true`, `diagnostics = true` for the XF box rows, `scale_percent = 100`, `theme` set per row, and `motion` set per row (`full` or `reduced`; `overlay_state` and the CET window's Diagnostics card say which is in force). The XF box shows only while it is the status surface: the HUD hidden (photo mode, a menu) or the ink panel off (`ui_hud {show: false}`), the CET overlay closed. Each capture is `capture_screenshot` (full resolution), with `capture_burst` where the row says motion. `ui_hud {cet_label: false}` keeps the CET line out of XF box captures. Start with `[overlay] enabled = true`, and switch the rest live with `overlay_set` (in memory; config.ini is untouched and a restart goes back to it). `theme`, `motion` and `diagnostics` apply at once: the XF box restyles before its next frame, and the CET window and line take the theme with their next info refresh (about 2 s; wait 3 s before a capture). `enabled: false` stops the XF box with its hooks kept, for the CET-only rows, and `enabled: true` brings it back. Switching on an overlay that was off at load is refused with `needs_restart` (its hooks attach only at load), so that is the one restart. Each answer's `undo` puts back the values it changed; `overlay_state` and `bridge_info` (`overlay`) show the values in force. Batch the dark rows, then `overlay_set {theme: "light"}`, then the light rows. The effective motion is `overlay.motion` in `XFBridge_Info` and `motion` in `overlay_state`.

**As a batch.** `tools/sessions/st-styling.json` runs every row below, dark then light, unattended (38 rows). The runner sets `[overlay] enabled` and `diagnostics` once before the launch; each row then sets its own look with `overlay_set` and checks `overlay_state` before its captures, so the only restart is the read-only one (after the dark Stopped row, whose kill needs it anyway; the light Stopped row ends the run). An `overlay_set` answering `needs_restart` falls back to config.ini and a restart. The teardown puts the live look back and restores config.ini. Before every capture a row verifies its named state through the bridge (`bridge_info`'s `status_surface`, `status.chip` and `bridge.writes_paused`, `overlay_state`'s `cet_open` and motion, `ui_state`'s waiting question; a stopped bridge's `killed` refusal) and fails with what was there instead; after it, `capture.surface` checks the surface's plate is in frame (every frame of a burst), and a burst starts only once a probe finds it. A row judged by XF · Studio's UI review ends **CAPTURED** (awaiting that review), never PASS. Paused is the CET window's own Pause changes (`session_handover` is not that pause), and the compact DIAGNOSTICS disclosure opens the native card without a person: both through the bridge's `cet.panel {press, diagnostics_open}` command. ST2's warning and danger tones need a test-only diagnostics figure (`overlay_set {diagnostics_cpu_ms}`); the overlay's own CPU time stays near 0.15 ms. No row needs the player: the runner refuses an ask in any row not marked `manual: true`, and the preflight lists the manual rows (none here). ST1 and ST2 show their question for the capture only. ST9's Windows half reads Windows' Animation effects setting and checks `overlay_state` agrees with it, without changing it. ST13's Reload all mods half is left out (CET has no scripted reload; run it by hand). ST10's alt-Enter runs only when the fixtures opt in (`allow_alt_enter: true`; absent or false skips it). Every plugin log the attempt writes is kept whole in the report folder's `logs/a<attempt>/`. `tools/sessions/st-rerun.json` is the re-run after scripted session 1's review packet: the rows whose captures didn't show their state (ST6 and ST8 paused, ST7 diagnostics, ST3's settle, ST2's tones), ST5, ST13, and the rows whose bursts began before their surface showed (ST4 changes, ST3 reduced, ST8 connected and auto); 20 rows, no restart.

| Row | Prepared action | Required evidence / pass boundary |
|---|---|---|
| ST1 — the XF box, dark, motion full | `theme = dark`, `motion = full`, `diagnostics = true`. Hide the HUD (photo mode, or `ui_hud {show: false}`), CET overlay closed. `ui_message ask: "Fully visible, or partly cut off?"` with two answers. `capture_screenshot`. | The box shows the heading (chamfered yellow mark with XF, the title in the display face, a cyan TEST chip), a status line with a pulsing dot and the plugin's sentence in the muted colour, the question and its keys (muted, in the label size), and under them the Diagnostics card: eyebrow, key-value rows (Frames, CPU, Back buffer, Frame generation, Bridge, Theme), a labelled bar "x.xx ms of 1 ms" with the figure readable across fill and track, and a thin moving bar. Five fonts, one per role, no blur. The corner of the TEST chip is cut (top right and bottom left). Nothing is clipped at 360 px (1080p) or at the player's scale. |
| ST2 — the XF box, light, motion full | The same with `overlay_set {theme: "light"}`. For the bar's tones, the test figure `overlay_set {diagnostics_cpu_ms: 0.6}`, then `0.8`, then `0.95` (a build with test hooks; `overlay_state` reports it as `cpu_ms` with `cpu_ms_override`), and `{diagnostics_cpu_ms: null}` after. | Same elements. Text and frames read on the cream plate (no white-on-white, no black plate). Border is the strong line; cards and the bar track are the soft line. The labelled bar's figure is readable in all three of: unfilled track, filled part, and the warning/danger tone (the test figure sets it: warning past 0.75 ms, danger past 0.9 ms; the CET window's bar takes the same figure with its next info refresh). |
| ST3 — the XF box, dark and light, motion reduced | `overlay_set {motion: "reduced"}` (live). The tone change for the settle check is `cet_panel {press: "pause_changes"}`, then `{press: "resume_changes"}`. Two captures 1.2 s apart (`capture_burst {frames: 6}` spanning the pulse's half period). | The dot is identical in every frame (alpha 1), no wash behind the status row when the tone changes (`cet_panel` presses Pause changes and Resume changes, as the CET window's button would), an indeterminate bar, if one shows, is a still 35 % segment. The thin diagnostics bar still moves (an instrument). `overlay_state.motion.effective` reads `reduced`, reason `setting`. |
| ST4 — the pulse, motion full | `capture_burst {frames: 12}` of the dot while connected and read-only, at the burst's frame rate noted. | The dot's alpha rises and falls smoothly once per 2.4 s between 1 and 0.6: no frame jumps by more than a few percent, size and colour are unchanged, nothing else pulses. `changes allowed` pulses too (amber); `Paused` shows an amber ring and is still; `Stopped` (red) is still. |
| ST5 — the settle | From read-only, `Pause changes` is not offered; use a `-writes` profile: `cet_panel {press: "pause_changes"}` and `{press: "resume_changes"}` (test builds with test hooks; the button's own action, run once after the CET layer's claim; `bridge_info` `cet_panel.last_press` says how it went, and a remote resume over a pause the player set is refused with `paused_by_player`); `capture_burst` at the change. | A cyan wash behind the status row at full strength on the change, gone within about 0.6 s, once; a second change within 2 s produces none. Under `reduced` none at all. |
| ST6 — the chips | Read-only, Changes allowed, Paused (`cet_panel {press: "pause_changes"}`, a test build with test hooks) and Stopped in turn (CET window row, and the XF box heading is always the TEST chip). Capture each; `bridge_info` `cet_panel.chip` is the chip the window drew. | Chip text is the plugin's own label in capitals, tracked, in the tone: Read-only green wash, Changes allowed amber wash, Paused amber outline with no fill, Stopped red. The border is the tone at half strength. The sentence beside the dot is the plugin's wording, unchanged. |
| ST7 — the CET window, dark and light | CET overlay open: running read-only, changes allowed with a three-answer question, answered, paused with Resume, stopped with Reconnect, and Diagnostics through `cet.panel {diagnostics_open: true}`. Capture both themes; verify `bridge_info.cet_panel` request/undo and pause receipts as before, plus `overlay.state.cet_diagnostics` and `drawing_now` for the native card. | Exactly one title: `XF Runtime Bridge 0.8.23` from the build, in the title bar; no content title or Version footer. XF mark, TEST and mode chip share the status row. Sections in order: Status (one line and Reconnect/Resume when applicable), Question when present (unchanged card and answers), Session (Pause/Resume, Mark), Saves (status and Save anyway when offered), Comment for XF Studio, Keys, Danger last. Every section has an eyebrow; 16 px between, 8 px within. Keys has no card. Pause, Mark and Save anyway have visible borders/fills in both themes. Inline hints share button text baselines. Comment is three lines with a real Send button at its right on the same row. Diagnostics opens the native XF card beside CET, with no long CET list; closing CET clears it. No drawing failures or unbalanced pushes; question reservation and the 0.4 s click guard still hold. |
| ST8 — the CET line, dark and light | CET overlay closed, XF box and ink panel off. Foreground screen captures connected (two 1.2 s apart), paused through `cet.panel {press: "pause_changes"}`, and stopped. | 12 px side / 8 px top-bottom inner padding and 16 px corner inset. Dot centred on text x-height in its own slot, 6 px before the text; connected pulses, paused is an amber ring, stopped red and still, reduced motion still. One title in label role: XF Runtime Bridge, followed by muted `Connected · changes allowed` / `Connected · changes paused`; no repeated XF. Stopped is exactly one row: `Stopped · reconnect in the CET window`; the longer explanation is confined to the window. Existing live question/answer hints remain below in label role. No chip, card or button on the line. |
| ST9 — the motion setting | `[overlay] motion` `auto`, then Windows' Settings > Accessibility > Visual effects > Animation effects off (revert it after). `overlay_state`, `XFBridge_Info` through the CET window's Diagnostics card. Then `motion = full` with the same Windows setting. | `auto` follows Windows within one info refresh (2 s): `motion.effective` `reduced`, reason `windows_animations_off`, the dot still. With frame generation loaded, `auto` reads `reduced`, reason `frame_generation`; `full` animates whatever either says; `reduced` stills it whatever either says. A bad value in config.ini keeps `auto` and logs one warning. |
| ST10 — fonts baked once | `overlay_state` before and after a resolution change (alt-Enter or the game's setting), several minutes of play. | `cpu_ms` stays at or under its earlier value except for the one frame after a resize that bakes the five fonts; `frames` keeps rising; `phase` stays `drawing`; the plugin log has no `overlay.stopped` about a texture upload. The text is as sharp after the resize as before it. |
| ST11 — the ink panel's answer flash | A waiting question on the ink panel with `answer_keys` set, then press a key that answers nothing (an unbound answer key) so the answers flash. `capture_burst {frames: 14}`. | The answers go red for about 0.45 s, then the yellow (blue under the pointer) comes back over about 0.25 s, rising from dim to full. One hold and one return; no alternation; about 2 changes in 0.7 s where the old flash made 6 in 0.9 s. Nothing else on the panel changes (no visual change otherwise). |
| ST12 — DisabledAlpha, height cap and scrollbars | Empty and filled comments in the CET window, in both themes; a waiting question and enough Keys notes to overflow, including a 1600 px display and a smaller display. Scroll to Danger and back; inspect the title and first action without scrolling. | Send is a right-aligned real button beside the three-line field, disabled at 45 % opacity when empty. Entire window is at most 70 % of display height; overflowing content scrolls internally without clipping, with the token 8 px scrollbar. Every section and Stop remain reachable. No repeated title/footer or long Diagnostics list. |
| ST13 — `cet_open` with the XF box off | `[overlay] enabled = false`. `overlay_state`, then open the CET overlay (Ctrl and the backquote key), `overlay_state`, close it, `overlay_state`. Once more after CET's Reload all mods with the overlay open. | `cet_open` is false, true, false: it follows CET's overlay whether or not the XF box is enabled (also after `overlay_set {enabled: false}`) (Borg 4 read false with the overlay open). After the reload with the overlay open it reads true (CET sends `onOverlayOpen` after `onInit`). It is null only before the CET layer has reported (no CET, or before its `onInit`). |

**Capture list for XF · Studio's UI review (28 captures plus 3 bursts).** Dark and light each: ST1 (XF box, diagnostics on), ST3 (XF box, reduced), ST6 (the four chips in the CET window), ST7 (CET window: running, question waiting, question answered, stopped with Reconnect, Diagnostics open), ST8 (CET line connected, paused, stopped); bursts: ST4 (dot pulse), ST5 (settle), ST11 (ink answer flash). Each capture is paired with the Studio's [In-game surfaces](../../projects/xf-studio/authoring/src/studio-ui/style-guide/in-game.ts) specimen for the same state at the same width, so the review can set them side by side.

**Unknowns this card settles:** that the CET layer's calls, which `tools/test/cet-bindings.test.ts` checks offline against CET 1.37.1's binding table read from `sol_imgui.h` (overloads, enum members, and CET's check of the calling function's environment), behave the same in the game (each failure is named once in the mod's log and that part draws plain); that the host's five fonts and the chamfered polygons look right at 1080p, 1440p and 4K; that `SPI_GETCLIENTAREAANIMATION` follows the Windows setting without a restart.

**Friction log prompts for the session.** Any state the surfaces cannot show without a restart (theme, motion), any glyph missing from a face, any control that had to be reached with a key the window does not name, and any capture that needed a manual step the bridge could have taken (hiding the HUD, opening a question).

## Borg 3 regressions (0.8.18)

**Source/offline, unrun.** Fixes for what the third Borg session found (25 October 2026, bridge 0.8.17). Offline tests cover each; the rows are the runtime confirmation. None needs a desktop key press, which is the point of rows B318-3 and B318-4: each desktop-control call can pull the Claude window over the game.

| Row | Prepared action | Required evidence / pass boundary |
|---|---|---|
| B318-1 — no invalid heartbeats | Start the recorder with all channels (or subtitles, voice, face, ui, contacts, v) for at least 2 minutes of play, then `test_record_read` / `game_catchup` and read the manifest's `channel_stats`. Once more starting it right after a load. | `invalid` is 0 on a clean run, a start right after a load included: a subtitles or voice coverage sample with `controller_count: 0` in the run's first 2 s, before the tap has been ready and with the rest of the tap healthy, is `reason: not_ready` and counted in `not_ready` (and `channel_stats.<channel>.not_ready`), not invalid; once the tap is ready, `channel_stats.<channel>.ready` is true and `tap_not_ready` is empty (a run that never got there has `complete_coverage: false`) (it was 15 to 25 percent: every subtitles, voice, face and ui coverage sample came back `error: failed`, because their answers lacked the script-call envelope). `channel_stats.<channel>.valid` equals `attempts` for the polled channels. A coverage sample that is genuinely invalid says why (`reason`, or the `error` and `message`). An event-armed `capture.arm` on `dm.quest` is no longer cancelled by `invalid_source` during plain play. |
| B318-2 — weapon state | `player_action weapon.draw`; read `player_state` / `game_digest` `weapon_state` and the recorder's `v` rows; then aim, fire once into the approved empty backstop, reload, `weapon.holster`. Repeat with a melee weapon (`weapon.arms` or a bat in slot 1). | `kind` says `ranged`, `melee` or `none`, `drawn` is true with a weapon in hand. A ready firearm reads `raw` 5 / `script_name` Ready (Safe 6, Reload 2, Shoot 8 as before), a melee weapon `melee_raw` and a gamePSMMeleeWeapon name (Idle 2 and so on). A drawn firearm at `raw` 0 is `NotReady` with `not_ready_blockers` (a status effect, the scene tier, a takedown ...): record which one, because that is the runtime answer to why 0.8.17 read 0. `unknown` appears only for a value outside the tables. |
| B318-3 — ring_s and auto_answer | `phone_call {contact, ring_s: 30, lines, auto_answer: false}` and leave it ringing for 20 s; then `phone_call {..., auto_answer: true}`. | The unanswered call rings for the asked time (the game's own 8 s timeout no longer misses it; the plugin log's `dm call ringing ... rings up to 30 s` line) and `dm.phone` says `missed` at about ring_s, not at 8 s. With `auto_answer` the phone rings, `dm.phone` `auto_answer` then `answered` follow within a second or so with no key press, the lines show, the call ends by itself. A game call (a quest's) keeps its own timeout. |
| B318-3b — a stale auto-answer is dropped | Place an `auto_answer: true` call, and `phone_end` it (or `bridge_kill`) in the same moment; once more, let a vanilla call or a second `phone_call` from the same contact replace it. | The plugin log has `a bridge pickup for <contact> was dropped (...)` for the stale pickup, and no call the bridge did not place is answered. The player's own answer key and a quest's pickup are unaffected. |
| B318-4 — call widget not covered | With the XF HUD panel shown (`ui_hud`), place an `auto_answer: false` call and capture the screen while it rings (`capture_screenshot`), then during the call. Read `ui_state` `phone_open`. | The XF panel is hidden while the incoming-call widget and the call are up (`phone_open` true), so the answer and reject hints and the avatar are visible; the panel returns when the call ends. |
| B318-5 — subtitle text | Repeat DA2 (the stand-in's `greeting`, or any line with an ellipsis) and read the subtitles through `game_catchup` using the CLI (`bun tools/bridge-client.ts run game.catchup ...`) and through the MCP. | The text reads `nap…` or its `…` escape in the CLI, never a replacement character; the recorder's chunk files are UTF-8 (they were already, byte for byte). If a replacement character still appears in the MCP or chunk output, record the exact path it came through: that would be a new finding, not this fix. |
| B318-6 — call speaker | `phone_call {contact: "coach", lines: [{text}]}` with no `speaker`. | The subtitle speaker is the contact's name as the phone shows it (Life Coach), not `coach`. A line with its own `speaker` keeps it. |
| B318-7 — catch-up counts | `game_catchup` over a span with the UI channel on. | `counts.ui` equals `count_detail.ui.invalid + coalesced + listed`, and `channels.ui` (plus `omitted.ui`) equals `listed`; polled snapshots are in `ui_latest` and counted under `coalesced`. |

## Concurrent readers (0.8.15)

**All rows unrun.** Coordinator records the reviewed `claude/bridge-multiclient` checkpoint (base 3d0dabfc), DLL SHA-256, isolated profile and config before staging the explicit autonomous -writes candidate with test hooks. Keep existing frameworks and the safety manual save; maintainer launches/operates the game. End by loading that save. No automatic deployment.

Use the session's existing private evidence root with a versioned `multiclient-<candidate-sha>/` child: redacted discovery (no token), native log, per-process timestamp/correlation/result/elapsed-ms logs, recorder manifest and row verdicts. Keep a friction log. For responsiveness R&D, measure writer p50/p95/max for the same ten short commands before and during observer load; record retries, timeouts, frame/tick counts and visible hitches.

Persistent bounded watcher, from the bridge project (one reader API for about 60 seconds):

```powershell
bun -e 'import {CommandApi} from "./tools/api/command-api.ts"; const a=new CommandApi({readOnly:true,idleCloseMs:60000}); try {for(let i=0;i<120;i++){const t=performance.now(); const r=await a.run("game.digest",{events:0,sky:false}); console.log(JSON.stringify({i,ms:performance.now()-t,...r})); await Bun.sleep(500);}} finally {a.close();}'
```

| Row | Prepared action | Required observation |
|---|---|---|
| MC1 | Log baseline writer MCP timings for ten short reads and approved reversible clock/notification commands, including undo. Start the watcher; keep MCP as writer; repeatedly run `bun tools/bridge-client.ts --read-only run game.digest` from another terminal while repeating the writer batch. | Three tools overlap without busy/timeout/hitch. Compare baseline/concurrent latency distribution. One MCP writer plus two readers is the intended arrangement. |
| MC2 | While MCP holds the writer, try default CLI `ping`; use reader CLI `--read-only call` for an approved clock write, `npc.pick` and `test.record.start`; confirm reader `npc.list` / `npc.status` still work. Try reader `game.saves` / `game.options.read`, then their writer versions. | Second writer gets `bridge_busy`; reader changes/leases/exclusive queries get `read_only_connection`, change nothing; writer queries still work. |
| MC3 | MCP starts a short source recording. Readers replay with different `since` cursors; close/reopen both readers while MCP continues commands and drains/stops recording. Then separately close the writer. | Reader cursors/acks are independent; reader disconnect never changes writer generation, stops recording or cancels writer input/supervisor leases. Writer loss still ends its run. Record manifest/cadence evidence. |
| MC4 | With a watcher, start three more persistent reader clients against the default pool; close one admitted reader and retry the fourth. Repeat with an idle reader. | At most three admitted; fourth busy; released slot reused. Idle reader has no effect on writer cursor/leases/recorder. No sustained thread/handle growth. |
| MC5 | Poll heavily from readers while MCP sends short commands. Reader issues `behave.stop` / `player.stop`, then `bridge.kill`; exercise the physical kill switch too. | Bounded reader pacing/refusals and sampled logs; reconnecting the same abusive endpoint retains its penalty. Repeated identical reader stops return coalesced admission receipts, with no completion claim. An abusive reader endpoint's emergency requests wait for its backoff; use the control pipe or kill key. Writer/control cancellation remains independent and responsive; writer queued steps take priority. kill closes all endpoints and restores/drains as before. In-game rearm creates fresh discovery; old tokens/endpoints cannot resume. |
| MC6 | Repeat three-tool overlap across an approved safety-save reload, then close all tools and review logs/thread/handle counts. | Load-detach gate protects every client; no crash, stale run continuation or off-thread game access. Observer reconnect cannot resurrect writer leases. Log all friction and unproven coverage. |
